Async hooks finish out of order: `PostToolBatch` was sometimes logged before the `PreToolUse` it follows, and several events shared one millisecond.
- **Pass criteria (plan E1):** Code Mode on, shown by a fixed screen line readable by a program; prompt as first turn; transcript with the given session ID; the `-n` name in the roster; the contract's code word known. **Passed.**
- **Not verified:** Linux on that CLI version (later Linux startup and holder checks are recorded in M114); the holder started by a real hub.
- **Current use:** the Code Mode start and its start check, which needs the `Code Mode (experimental) is on` line (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Command and Start check). Re-run M63, M65, M70 and M71 after a Claude Code upgrade, since Code Mode is experimental and may change in any release.

## M64. Skipping the gateway for a Code Mode session

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300. The operator's `~/.claude/settings.json` `env` sets `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to a local gateway and `_CLAUDE_CODE_ASSUME_FIRST_PARTY_BASE_URL`; it has no `apiKeyHelper`.
- **Experiment:** promptless starts through the M63 holder with `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE=1` and the process environment's `ANTHROPIC_*` variables removed, varying the overrides; then `/status`. Credential cases put the variable in the scratch repository's `.claude/settings.local.json`, which ranks above user settings and below `--settings`.
- **Result:**

  | Overrides | Code Mode |
  |---|---|
  | none | off: `isn't available: for now it only works directly with the Anthropic API (not through a cloud provider such as Bedrock, a gateway or another address in ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL). Starting with the ordinary tools.`; `/status` shows the gateway address |
  | `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` in the process environment only | off, the same line: user settings `env` wins |
  | `--settings {"env":{"ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL":"https://api.anthropic.com"}}` | on; `/status`: login by the Claude account, base URL `https://api.anthropic.com` |
  | that, with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in settings | on, but a start prompt "Detected a custom API key in your environment. Do you want to use this API key?" |
  | that, with `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` in settings | off: `isn't available: for now it doesn't work with an API key helper, ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN or custom auth headers.` |
  | that, with `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS: "X-Api-Key: …"` in settings | off: `isn't available: Claude Code couldn't read your organization's settings or policy (starting Claude Code again may help).` |
  | that, with `"ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": ""`, `"ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN": ""` and `"ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS": ""` added to `--settings` `env`, against each of the three above | on; `/status`: login by the Claude account, base URL `https://api.anthropic.com` |
  | `apiKeyHelper` in settings, `--settings` `"apiKeyHelper": ""` | off, the API key helper line |
  | `apiKeyHelper` in settings, `--settings` `"apiKeyHelper": null` | settings error dialog: `apiKeyHelper: Expected string, but received null` |

  `_CLAUDE_CODE_ASSUME_FIRST_PARTY_BASE_URL` from user settings did not stop Code Mode once the base URL was overridden.
- **Pass criteria (plan E2):** one combination starts Code Mode with `/status` showing the direct API and the subscription sign-in, also when settings set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. **Passed:** `--settings` `env` with the base URL and the three credential variables as empty strings. The process environment needs no override.
- **Not verified:** `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in user settings themselves (local settings stood in); an `Authorization` header; a managed-settings gateway. An `apiKeyHelper` in user settings cannot be overridden by `--settings`, so it keeps Code Mode off.
- **Current use:** the `--settings` `env` override that skips the gateway (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Environment).

## M65. Hook events in a Code Mode session

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Experiment:** an M63 session with an extra synchronous `PreToolUse` hook on `Bash|PowerShell` (timeout 3 s) that exits 1 with stderr for one command, sleeps 10 s for another and returns `updatedInput` for a third; an async `PreToolUse` hook that always exits 1; and a synchronous `SessionStart` hook on `compact` that returns `additionalContext` with a second code word. Turns: four Bash calls from code; `AskUserQuestion`; a permission prompt in manual mode (reached with Shift+Tab); long-running code; `/compact`; two concurrent `REPL` calls in auto mode.
- **Result:**
  - A synchronous `PreToolUse` hook that exited 1 did not stop the call: the command ran. One that ran past its timeout refused it with `Permission denied for Bash: PreToolUse:Bash hook error: [node]: the hook did not finish, so this call was not run.`, and the refused call had no `PostToolUse` or `PostToolUseFailure`. `updatedInput` replaced the command. The failing async hook never refused a call.
  - `AskUserQuestion` exists in Code Mode and waits as in M57: `PreToolUse` `REPL`, `PermissionRequest` with `tool_name: "AskUserQuestion"`, then `Notification` `permission_prompt` 6 s later; the roster said `status: "waiting", waitingFor: "input needed"`. After the answer: `PostToolUse` `AskUserQuestion`, `poll_event`, `Stop`.
  - A permission prompt for `Bash` from code: `PreToolUse` `Bash`, `PermissionRequest` with `tool_name: "Bash"`, `Notification` `permission_prompt` 6 s later; roster `waitingFor: "permission prompt"`.
  - Code does not outlive its turn. While a `REPL` script runs, the turn stays running (`esc to interrupt`, `1 background task` on the status line, roster `busy`), and `Stop` follows the script's end; every `Stop` had `background_tasks: []`. A script's own time is capped at 30 s, so a 45-second `sleep()` in code ended at 30 s; an un-awaited timer dies when its script ends; `Bash` with `run_in_background: true` is refused with `run_in_background is not available in the async REPL`. A 50-second shell command awaited in code ran to its end inside the turn. In auto mode each code result reached the model as `UserPromptSubmit` `poll_event` before the turn's `Stop`. In the manual-mode turn whose `Bash` call waited on a permission prompt, `PostToolUse` `REPL` and `PostToolBatch` were followed by `Stop` with no `poll_event`.
  - `/compact` gave `PreCompact`, `SubagentStop`, then `SessionStart` `compact` and no `Stop`. The `compact` hook's `additionalContext` reached the model: after compaction it named both code words.
  - Two `REPL` calls in one reply both started, but their `Bash` calls ran one after the other: the second call's `PreToolUse` came 60 ms after the first call's 25-second command ended. No prompt was raised; this run did not exercise tool calls that overlap.
- **States:** running is `UserPromptSubmit` (either source), `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse` or `PostToolBatch`; a question or permission wait is `PermissionRequest` and its `Notification`, told apart by the request's tool (M57); idle is `Stop`, or `SessionStart` `compact` after `/compact`; ended is `SessionEnd` (M68). A turn can end without a `poll_event`.
- **Pass criteria (plan E3):** each state has an identifying marker sequence; background code is visible so idle is not reported while code runs; a `poll_event` turn ends with `Stop`; Switchboard's async `PreToolUse` entry never refuses a call; the contract survives compaction. **Partial.** Passed: running, a question or permission wait, idle and ended each have an identifying sequence; the turn stays open until code ends, so idle is not reported while code runs; each `poll_event` turn ended with `Stop`; the async `PreToolUse` entry never refused a call; the contract survived compaction. Outstanding: the failed state, since no `StopFailure` was produced.
- **Not verified:** `StopFailure` (an unknown model in `/model` was refused before any request); the auto-mode prompt with a two-minute countdown, which needs tool calls that overlap; `Elicitation`; whether output can arrive after `Stop` by another route, such as `Monitor`.
- **Superseded in part by M78, M79 and M80:** code can outlive its turn, and a `Stop` then lists it in `background_tasks` (M78 for a background shell, M80 for code); the failed state is `StopFailure` (M79); the countdown prompt is recorded in M80.
- **Current use:** the marker mapping of held sessions' states (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Liveness and state).

## M66. Reports from a Code Mode session

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; the installed `hub.js` bundle copied to a scratch home with a stand-in hub that answered `GET /status` and recorded requests.
- **Experiment:** the M63 session ran `node <home>/.switchboard/hub.js report stage …` with the `Bash` tool and `report worktree … /d/git/x` with the `PowerShell` tool, both from code, and printed `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` and `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH`.
- **Result:** both commands printed `recorded`; each `POST /report` carried the session's ID as `nativeSessionId`. Both shells saw `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` and `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH`, and Bash also `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE=1` and the overridden base URL. From PowerShell, `/d/git/x` arrived as `D:\d\git\x`, as for any PowerShell call (M20 concerns Git Bash only).
- **Pass criteria (plan E4):** the stand-in receives the report with the right session ID. **Passed.**
- **Current use:** held sessions send reports with the unchanged report command and contract (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions).

## M67. The roster, `claude stop` and `claude attach` for a held session

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Result:** `claude agents --json` and `--all` list a held interactive session as `{pid, cwd, kind: "interactive", startedAt, sessionId, name, status}` with `status` `busy`, `idle` or `waiting` and, when waiting, `waitingFor` `input needed` or `permission prompt`; it has no short `id`. `claude stop` and `claude attach` with the first 8 characters, the full ID or the name print `No session matching '<arg>'. Run 'claude agents' to list running sessions.` and exit 1, and leave the session running. After `/clear`, `/branch` or `/resume` the entry keeps the pid and shows the new session ID and name (M73).
- **Pass criteria (plan E5):** recorded either way. **Passed.**
- **Current use:** the roster read skips processes the holder holds, so a held session is never classed `interactive` (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions).

## M68. Resuming a Code Mode session after its process ends

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Experiment:** the M65 session was killed with `taskkill /T /F` 8 s into a turn running `sleep 30`, then resumed in a new pseudo-terminal with `claude --resume <id> --permission-mode auto --model haiku --settings <same>`, a new `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH`, with no prompt and then with one; then once without `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE`.
- **Result:**
  - The kill left no `SessionEnd` and no `Stop`: Switchboard's marker stayed at `PostToolBatch`, which reads as running. The hook payloads logged with the killed launch's `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH` tell it from later launches.
  - A promptless resume showed the Code Mode line, the conversation and the `-n` name; its only hook was `SessionStart` `resume` with the same session ID and the new launch, and the roster said `idle`. Model and permission mode were as passed. The interrupted turn did not continue.
  - A resume with a prompt ran it as a turn: `SessionStart` `resume`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`. The model knew both code words and that the killed command's result was lost.
  - Without the variable Claude printed `This session was started in Code Mode (experimental), which is off, so it can't be resumed. To resume it, set the environment variable CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE to 1, then run claude --resume <id>.` and exited 1.
- **Pass criteria (plan E6):** same session ID, conversation and contract; Code Mode on; model and permission mode settable; a promptless resume reads as idle; the stale marker of a killed process distinguishable by its launch ID; the refusal recorded. **Passed**, with the launch ID taken from the hook's environment, which the hook writer does not record yet. The resume passed no `--append-system-prompt`, so the contract seen came from the conversation.
- **Later:** the hook writer records the launch in each marker of a Code Mode process (`packages/claude/src/hooks.ts`), and a resume appends the panel, link and conversation sections (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Resume).
- **Current use:** the resume of a held session and the launch ID in its markers (`CodeModeRunner.resumeHeld`, architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Resume and Liveness and state).

## M69. Held sessions when the holder dies

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Experiment:** the holder held three Code Mode sessions: two idle and one running the Bash command `sleep 40; echo e7done > e7done.txt` from code. The holder process alone was killed with `taskkill /PID <holder> /F` 25 s into the command.
- **Result:** every `claude` and the members of its process tree seen before the kill were gone within 5 s. Each session logged `SessionEnd` with `reason: "other"` 1 to 2 s after the kill, so Switchboard's hook writer removed its marker, and the roster no longer listed it. The shell command did not stop: `e7done.txt` was written 15 s after its `claude` had exited. Its `sleep` was not under `claude.exe` in the process table, so neither the tree nor the roster shows it.
- **Pass criteria (plan E7):** each `claude` exits within 30 s, or a survivor is detectable. **Passed** for `claude`. A tool's shell command can outlive its session unseen.
- **Not verified:** Linux and `SIGKILL`; whether `taskkill /T` on `claude` (M68) also leaves such a command running.
- **Current use:** the holder-loss rule and the resume guard (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions).

## M70. Prompts before a Code Mode session starts

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Experiment:** a start in a new folder with a project `.mcp.json` server, on a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` holding copies of the operator's sign-in, `.claude.json` without `codeModeCaveatsAcceptedV1` and `codeModeAutoModeCaveatAcceptedV1`, and settings without hooks, with `--session-id` and a prompt.
- **Result:** the dialogs came one after another, each answered by keys sent to the pseudo-terminal:
  1. Folder trust ("Accessing workspace: … Quick safety check: Is this a project you created or one you trust?"), with `❯ No, exit` selected, naming `.mcp.json` when there is one.
  2. "New MCP server found in this project: e8dummy", `❯ Continue without using this MCP server` selected.
  3. "Allow external CLAUDE.md file imports?", because the folder's parent `CLAUDE.md` imports `AGENTS.md`; `❯ No, disable external imports` selected.
  4. "Code Mode (experimental)", with `❯ No, start without it` selected, so Enter starts without Code Mode.

  No hook fired until all four were answered; then `SessionStart` `startup` with the given session ID, the prompt as the first turn, and `Stop`. The pseudo-terminal's process kept its pid; a second `claude`-level process appeared under it. One answer saved both caveats to `.claude.json`. M63's scratch repository asked for trust and imports at its first start only. An API key in settings adds its own dialog (M64).
- **Pass criteria (plan E8):** a start prompt is detectable without a marker; the confirmation keeps the session ID and prompt. **Passed:** a live process with no marker of its launch is waiting on a dialog, and the prompt and session ID survived.
- **Side effect:** killing a session while its dialogs showed made the next Claude start on the machine print `Claude Code's fullscreen renderer didn't finish starting last time on this machine, so this launch is using the classic renderer.`; that start used the classic renderer once.
- **Not verified:** the name of the second process; what the operator's terminal shows after an attach mid-dialog.
- **Current use:** the start prompt rule of the start check (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Start check).

## M71. Starts that Claude makes without Code Mode

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Result:** with the M64 overrides, `--model claude-sonnet-5` started without Code Mode, printing `⚠ Code Mode (experimental) isn't available: for now it only works with Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5 and later models. Starting with the ordinary tools.` `DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1` printed `⚠ Code Mode (experimental) isn't available: it needs settings that Claude Code downloads from Anthropic, and this session doesn't download them (DISABLE_TELEMETRY, DO_NOT_TRACK, CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC or CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_FEATURE_FLAGS turns that off). Starting with the ordinary tools.` In both, `/status` showed `Code Mode: off · <reason>` and the `is on` line was absent. Haiku 5.5 runs Code Mode though the message names only Opus and Sonnet. Every refusal line in M64 and here starts `⚠ Code Mode (experimental) isn't available:` and wraps at the terminal's width.
- **Pass criteria (plan E9):** both lines recorded. **Passed.**
- **Current use:** the start check fails on the `isn't available` line (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Start check).

## M72. Replaying and attaching to a held session

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; @xterm/addon-serialize 0.14.0.
- **Experiment:** the holder kept each session in `@xterm/headless` (10,000 lines of scrollback) with `SerializeAddon`, and a raw output buffer. Replays of each were written into fresh headless terminals and compared with the holder's screen. Attach clients connected over loopback TCP, sent their size, received the replay and the output stream, and sent keys: a headless client, and a raw-mode Node.js client (`process.stdin.setRawMode`) running inside a second ConPTY, standing in for Windows Terminal and VS Code's terminal.
- **Result:**
  - At the session's size both replays reproduced the visible screen and scrollback exactly, and re-serializing the replay gave the same bytes. Replayed at another size without a resize, both were garbled.
  - The holder resizing the pseudo-terminal to the client's size before the replay made Claude redraw at that size, so a 90-column client showed a clean screen. The raw-mode client's terminal resize reached Claude.
  - Through both clients, a bracketed paste appeared in the input box, Shift+Tab cycled the mode (manual, accept edits, plan, auto), Ctrl+O opened the detailed transcript and Esc closed it; Ctrl+U cleared the box.
  - Attaching mid-turn showed the spinner and `esc to interrupt`; detaching left the turn running, and attaching after it showed the result.
  - With two clients of 120 and 80 columns, the later one set the size and the wider client showed lines drawn for 80 columns until it resized.
  - A serialized replay of 10,040 lines of 106 characters was 1,094,259 bytes, and the raw buffer 2,180,075.
- **Pass criteria (plan E10):** one replay method shows the screen as Claude drew it in both terminals, and keys reach Claude unchanged. **Passed.** With the headless client and the raw-mode client in a second ConPTY, serialization after a resize to the client's size reproduced the screen, and keys reached Claude. The replay, typing, Shift+Tab and Esc in Windows Terminal and in VS Code's terminal passed an eye check in live acceptance on 2026-11-19 (M84); paste and Ctrl+O were checked only through the raw-mode client.
- **Not verified:** Linux, where M114 later passed attach replay; mouse input (see M101 for mouse modes in the replay); a raw buffer that has dropped its start.
- **Current use:** the holder's replay, a serialized screen after a resize to the client's size (architecture.md § Terminal holder).

## M73. Session ID changes inside a held session

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Result:** in a held session started with `--session-id`:
  - `/clear`: `SessionEnd` of the old ID, then `SessionStart` with `source: "clear"` and a new ID, from the same process and the same `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH`. A report afterwards sent the new ID; the roster showed the same pid with the new ID.
  - `/fork` is refused: `This session is in Code Mode (experimental), which background sessions can't use yet, so it can't be moved or copied to the background.` No hook fired.
  - `/branch` printed `Branched session. You're now in the new one (<id>)`: `SessionEnd` of the old ID, `SessionStart` `source: "fork"` with the new one.
  - `/resume <other id>`: `SessionEnd`, `SessionStart` `source: "resume"` with the other ID; the roster then showed the other session's ID and name for the same pid.

  Since Switchboard's hook writer removes a marker on `SessionEnd`, each change removes the old ID's marker and writes one for the new ID.
- **Pass criteria (plan E12):** each ID change is detectable from markers carrying the launch ID. **Passed**, given a hook writer that records `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH`; nested `claude -p` markers also carry the launch ID, so the source of the change must be kept (M77).
- **Current use:** the identity rule that follows `/clear`, `/branch` and `/resume` (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions).

## M74. Debrief forks of a Code Mode session

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Result:** `claude -p --resume <Code Mode session> --fork-session` with `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE=1` printed `Warning: Code Mode (experimental) isn't available: for now it only works in the interactive terminal.` and, with or without the variable, `Failed to resume session: This session was started in Code Mode (experimental), which isn't available because for now it only works in the interactive terminal, so it can't be resumed here.` and exited 1.
- **Pass criteria (plan E13):** recorded. **Passed:** refused.
- **Current use:** debriefs skip Code Mode sessions (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions).

## M75. Memory of held sessions and how to read it

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; 643 processes on the machine.
- **Result:**
  - A Code Mode `claude.exe`: 395 to 399 MB working set and 741 to 757 MB private while idle for 7 minutes, 433 and 839 MB in a turn. Its tree also runs the operator's stdio MCP servers from user settings (two here, about 300 MB), about 700 MB working set and 950 MB private in all.
  - The prototype holder: about 100 MB working set with one or two sessions; one session's 10,000-line scrollback added about 110 MB working set and 140 MB private.
  - An idle session wrote nothing to its pseudo-terminal for 450 s.
  - Reading memory: `tasklist /FO CSV` 1.3 s; `pwsh -NoProfile -Command` with `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` 1.4 s (0.5 s in a running PowerShell); `Get-Process` with `.Parent` 22.6 s; `wmic` absent. In a running PowerShell, `Get-Process` of every process took 16 ms and `Get-Process -Id` of 20 pids 190 ms. PowerShell started in 450 ms.
- **Sampled read (2026-11-18, the holder's `MemorySampler`):** one `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` query in Windows PowerShell returning pid, parent, creation time and working set of 630 processes took 828 to 863 ms over three runs, and summing 20 process trees from it 1.5 to 2.8 ms (`.wip/code-mode-exp/memsample.mjs`).
- **Pass criteria (plan E14):** a method under 100 ms per list of 20 sessions; numbers for the idle limit. **Partial** here: `list` answers from the sampled read, under 3 ms for 20 trees, and the memory numbers cover 7 minutes. The hour-long run is M82, which passes E14.
- **Not verified:** Linux `/proc`. Growth over an hour is M82.
- **Current use:** the terminal holder's `list`, which reports each session's tree memory from a background read of the process table (`MemorySampler`, architecture.md § Terminal holder).

## M76. node-pty from a packaged extension

- **Historical:** the VS Code extension and its VSIX were removed on 2026-11-24; apps/hub's standalone build, installed by `npm run install-local`, now carries the holder with node-pty's files (`apps/hub/carried.cjs`). The results on loading node-pty from a copied folder still hold.
- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** node-pty 1.1.0, @vscode/vsce from the repository; Node.js 24.14.0 and VS Code 1.146.0's Electron 43.8.6 in Node mode; Windows 11 x64.
- **Experiment:** a probe extension whose `holder/` folder held the holder script, `@xterm/headless`, `@xterm/addon-serialize` and node-pty's `package.json`, `lib/**/*.js` without tests (including `worker/conoutSocketWorker.js` and `conpty_console_list_agent.js`) and `prebuilds/win32-x64` without `.pdb` files (`conpty.node`, `conpty_console_list.node`, `pty.node`, `winpty.dll`, `winpty-agent.exe`, `conpty/conpty.dll`, `conpty/OpenConsole.exe`). `vsce package --no-dependencies` included the nested `node_modules` (42 files, 1.78 MB). The VSIX was unzipped under the temp folder, away from any checkout, and a script spawned a pseudo-terminal from it, resized it from 80×24 to 132×40 and killed it.
- **Result:**
  - Spawn, resize (the child read 132×40) and kill worked under Node.js 24 (ABI 137) and under Electron 43 (ABI 148), since node-pty's prebuilds use N-API.
  - The kill printed `Error: AttachConsole failed` from `conpty_console_list_agent.js` on stderr and still ended the child.
  - With `conpty.node` removed, the holder started and listened, and only a spawn failed: `Failed to load native module: conpty.node, checked: build/Release, build/Debug, prebuilds/win32-x64: …`.
  - While a process had `.node` files loaded, Node.js `writeFileSync` and `copyFileSync` over one failed with `EBUSY`, and `rmSync` and `renameSync` over one with `EPERM`. A second copy of the build in another folder ran beside the first.
- **Pass criteria (plan E11):** spawn, resize and kill from the extracted folder on each platform; a running holder survives a new build installed beside it; a missing binary fails only a Code Mode start. **Partial.** Passed on Windows x64: spawn, resize and kill from the extracted folder under Node.js 24 and Electron 43; a second build in its own folder ran beside a running one, whose loaded `.node` files cannot be replaced; with `conpty.node` missing the holder started and only a spawn failed. Outstanding: Node.js 22, and the hub started with the binary missing. Linux x64 is deferred (plan Q8). Node.js 22 later passed in M81.
- **Current use:** the holder's artifact with node-pty's helpers and prebuilt binaries in apps/hub's standalone build, installed per build (architecture.md § Terminal holder).

## M77. Claude runs nested in a Code Mode session

- **Date:** 2026-11-18.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Experiment:** a held session's `Bash` tool printed its environment and ran `claude -p` and `claude --bg`, each with `--settings` naming a hook that logs its environment.
- **Result:**
  - The shell had `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE=1`, `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=https://api.anthropic.com`.
  - `claude -p` printed `Warning: Code Mode (experimental) isn't available: for now it only works in the interactive terminal.` and answered. Its hooks had a new session ID, `SessionStart` `startup`, the parent's `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH` and the variable, and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` set to the gateway: user settings `env` replaced the inherited direct address.
  - `claude --bg` started a background session (stopped with `claude stop`) whose hooks had no `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH`, no variable and the gateway address, since the background service starts it.
- **Pass criteria (plan E15):** nested runs work, and their markers cannot be read as the held session's or as its ID change. **Partial.** Nested runs work and their session IDs differ from the held session's. A `claude -p` run's hooks carry the held session's launch ID, and only its first event is `SessionStart` `startup`; the hook writer replaces the marker on each event, so its later markers look like those of a held process whose ID changed (M73). Telling them apart needs evidence that outlives the next event, such as a `SessionStart` source the marker keeps, or the roster listing the held pid with the new ID (M67).
- **Not verified:** whether the roster lists a nested `claude -p` run.
- **Later:** the hook writer keeps the `SessionStart` source of a Code Mode process's session ID in its marker (`startSource`), and an ID change counts only when the roster lists the held process with the new ID (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Identity).
- **Current use:** the identity rule, under which a nested run's markers change nothing (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions).

## M78. What ends a Code Mode session's shell commands

- **Date:** 2026-11-19.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.328 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; Node.js 24.14.0, node-pty 1.1.0 (Windows ConPTY, not `conpty.dll`), @xterm/headless 6.0.0; Git for Windows bash 5.2.37 (MSYS).
- **Experiment:** a driver (`.wip/code-mode-exp/exit/run-case.mjs`) spawned `claude` in a node-pty pseudo-terminal of 120×40 as in M63, with the M64 `--settings` overrides and a hook logger, on a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, in `.wip/mech`. The prompt had one `REPL` call run a Bash command: `sleep 40; echo done > <marker>` awaited in the turn (foreground); `echo start; sleep 40; echo done > <marker>` through `$` with `.timeout(3000)`, which moved it to the background ("Running in the background", `1 shell still running`) before the turn's `Stop` (background); and `node -e "setTimeout(() => writeFileSync(<marker>), 40000)"` awaited in the turn (native). `run_in_background` is refused in Code Mode (M65). Once the command showed in `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`, the driver read the process table, ended the session one way, then polled each process every 250 ms and checked the marker until 50 s after the command started.
- **Processes while the command runs:** `claude.exe` → `Git\bin\bash.exe` → `Git\usr\bin\bash.exe`, which has its own `conhost.exe` → for a native program, a further `bash.exe` → `node.exe`; all under `claude.exe`, background shells too. In every saved process snapshot, `sleep.exe` had a parent PID that no running process had, so it was outside `claude`'s tree. Two tree-kill runs, one background and one native, also read the pseudo-console's process list (node-pty's `_getConsoleProcessList`): both held only `claude.exe` and the list agent, so the shell runs in its own hidden console, not the one the holder owns.
- **Result:** process times are the first poll that found the process gone. Polling starts after the action returns; the `/exit` times are from the confirming Enter. "Command" is the shells and any native child; `sleep.exe` is listed separately.

  | Ending | `claude` | Command | `sleep.exe` | Marker | `SessionEnd` |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `/exit` typed, then Enter on its dialog | exit 0, 1.8–2.1 s | gone within 0.8 s | ran to its end | not written | `prompt_input_exit` |
  | Ctrl+C in a turn | stays, turn interrupted | gone at the first check, 2.5 s later | ran to its end | not written | none |
  | two Ctrl+C 300 ms apart at the prompt | exit 0; 1.4 s with no background work | gone (background shell: after the dialog) | ran to its end | not written | `prompt_input_exit` or `other` |
  | pseudo-console closed (`ClosePseudoConsole`) | exit 129, 1.8–2.1 s | gone within 0.8 s | ran to its end | not written | `other` |
  | node-pty `kill()` | exit 1, 0.1–0.3 s | **ran on** | ran to its end | **written** | none |
  | `taskkill /T /F` on `claude` | exit 1, 0.7–0.9 s | gone with it | ran to its end | not written | none |
  | `taskkill /F` on `claude` | exit 1, 0.7–0.8 s | **ran on** | ran to its end | **written** | none |

  - `/exit` while a turn or a background shell runs opens "Background work is running / The following will stop when you exit:", with `❯ 1. Exit and stop tasks` and `2. Stay`. It listed `code · <REPL description>` and `shell · <Bash description>` during the foreground turn, and only `shell · <command>` after the background turn. Nothing stops until it is answered: left open, the foreground command ran to its end, wrote the marker and the turn finished behind the dialog. Enter took the first choice.
  - The first Ctrl+C in a turn printed `Interrupted · What should Claude do instead?` and ended the command, with `PostToolUse` `REPL` and no `Stop`; the session stayed. At the prompt, single presses 2.5 s apart never exited, and two presses 300 ms apart exited, `SessionEnd` 0.15 s after the second. With a background shell the pair opened the same dialog and left the command running; another pair 300 ms apart exited, with `SessionEnd` `other` and the pseudo-terminal's exit 2.8 s after its first key.
  - Closing the pseudo-console gave the same cleanup when the holder had set both sockets' `readable` to false before the close, and when it disposed its output reader right after the close; closing after disposing the reader was not tried. node-pty's `kill()` starts an asynchronous query of the pseudo-console's process list, closes the pseudo-console and disposes its reader, then calls `process.kill` on each listed PID, here `claude`; `claude` died within 0.3 s, before its cleanup could run.
  - With a background shell running, `Stop` carried `background_tasks: [{id, type: "shell", status: "running", description, command}]`; M65's empty lists were turns without one.
  - Claude's own Bash timeout ended a command that begins with `sleep` (`Command timed out after 3s`, exit 143) the same way: the shells ended and `sleep.exe` ran on.
- **Answers:** in these runs a confirmed `/exit`, Ctrl+C twice at the prompt and a closed pseudo-console each ended the tool command's shells, the foreground `node.exe` included, and logged `SessionEnd`; Ctrl+C in a turn ended the command and kept the session. A kill of `claude` alone left the whole command running. `taskkill /T /F` ended the tree but logged no `SessionEnd`, so the marker stays as in M68. M69's holder death also logged `SessionEnd` `other` yet left its command running, so a holder that dies does not give the cleanup a closed pseudo-console gives; why is not known. No ending stopped the detached `sleep.exe`; ending its shells stopped what the command would have run after it. These results do not show that PRD Q10's job object is unnecessary: it is the only candidate for a holder that dies, a kill of `claude` alone and a process outside the tree.
- **Not verified:** Linux; a native program in a background shell; holder death with a native command; the PowerShell tool; how `sleep.exe` left the tree, including whether MSYS `exec` caused it, and whether a job object assigned at launch would contain it; commands with their own child trees, such as `dotnet build` servers.
- **Current use:** how the holder ends a session: it closes the pseudo-console, with a tree kill as the fallback (architecture.md § Ending a session). PRD Q10's job object is deferred.
- **Check of the built holder (2026-11-19):** it ended a held Code Mode session running `sleep 40; echo done > <marker>` by closing its pseudo-console: `kill` answered exit 129 after 1.9 s with no tree kill, the shells were gone, the marker was never written and `SessionEnd` `other` was logged.

## M79. A failed turn in a Code Mode session (E3, `StopFailure`)

- **Date:** 2026-11-19.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.328 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; Node.js 24.14.0, node-pty 1.1.0.
- **Experiment:** `/model claude-haiku-5-5-20991231` in a running session, to fail the next request with an unknown model. Then a session started with `--settings` `env` also setting `HTTPS_PROXY` and `HTTP_PROXY` to a local CONNECT proxy and `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_RETRIES=1`; after a turn that ended normally, the proxy was stopped (connection refused) and a prompt sent; the proxy was restarted to tunnel and a prompt sent; then a turn whose code ran `sleep 20` through `$`, with the proxy switched to refuse every tunnel with `502` while the command ran. Each hook payload was logged (`logs/fail2-hooks.jsonl`); screens and `claude agents --json` were read live and not saved.
- **Result:**
  - `/model` with the unknown ID printed `Model 'claude-haiku-5-5-20991231' not found`, sent no request and fired no hook, so it cannot produce a failed turn.
  - Code Mode stayed on with `HTTPS_PROXY` set; the proxy saw Claude's requests to `api.anthropic.com`, MCP and telemetry hosts.
  - Failure on the turn's first request: `UserPromptSubmit`, then `StopFailure` 0.9 s later and no `Stop`. The payload had `error: "server_error"`, `last_assistant_message: "API Error: Connection refused — a firewall or proxy may be blocking it (ECONNREFUSED)"`, `prompt_id` and `effort`, and no `background_tasks`. The screen showed `● API Error: Connection refused — a firewall or proxy may be blocking it (ECONNREFUSED)` and the input box's placeholder `Resend your message to try again`. The transcript's last entry was an `assistant` message with `isApiErrorMessage: true` and `error: "server_error"`. `claude agents --json` said `status: "idle"`.
  - Failure after code ran: `UserPromptSubmit`, `PreToolUse` `REPL`, `PreToolUse` `Bash`, `PostToolBatch`, `PostToolUse` `Bash`, `PostToolUse` `REPL`, then `StopFailure` 1.3 s after the code ended, with `error: "server_error"` and `API Error: Couldn't connect through your proxy (ERR_PROXY_TUNNEL) — the proxy refused the tunnel: check its credentials and that it allows this host`. No `poll_event` and no `Stop`. With one retry the proxy saw three tunnel requests over 1.2 s.
  - Every event carried the session ID and the hook process saw `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH`.
  - 60 s after the `StopFailure` a `Notification` `idle_prompt` fired. Switchboard's `Notification` matcher (`permission_prompt|worker_permission_prompt|agent_needs_input`, `packages/claude/src/hooks.ts`) does not install that one, so the `StopFailure` marker stays.
  - The next prompt after the proxy tunnelled again gave `UserPromptSubmit` and `Stop` as usual; the session needed no restart.
- **States:** a failed turn ends in `StopFailure` in place of `Stop`, both before any code and after code has run; with Switchboard's matchers it is the last marker until the next `UserPromptSubmit`, so `markerState` reads it as `failed` with `error` as the detail. For a connection failure `error` is only `server_error`; the readable cause is in `last_assistant_message`.
- **Pass criteria (plan E3, failed state):** the failed state has an identifying marker sequence. **Passed.** With the M65 entry, every state E3 names now has one.
- **Not verified:** other error classes (rate limit, authentication, invalid request, output-token limit) and their `error` values; the default retry count and how long it takes before `StopFailure`; a failure while background code is still running or on a `poll_event` delivery.
- **Current use:** `StopFailure` reads as `failed`, with `error` and Claude's `last_assistant_message` as the detail (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Liveness and state).

## M80. The auto-mode permission prompt with a countdown in a Code Mode session (E3)

- **Date:** 2026-11-19.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.328 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; Node.js 24.14.0, node-pty 1.1.0.
- **Experiment:** in auto mode, a first `REPL` script ``await $`sleep 50`; await $`rm -f <scratch file>`; "first done"``; while it ran, a message typed into the session asked for a second script, ``"marker-second"; await $`git status --short | head -3`; "second done"``, which a synchronous `PreToolUse` hook on `REPL` answered with `permissionDecision: "ask"`; that prompt was answered Yes, and the first script's `rm` then reached its permission check. The countdown prompt was left unanswered. Earlier attempts, which raised no countdown: two `REPL` calls in one reply; the second code in a later step; the second code sent after a typed message while the first ran; the first code left running past its turn's `Stop` with new code in the next turn, where the first script's later calls (`rm`, `curl`) left no call record and a later call got `This tool call did not run. The code that made it finished or was stopped before the call could start.`; and the second script sent while the same hook answered `permissionDecision: "allow"`. Screens and `claude agents --json` were read live and not saved; hook payloads (`logs/cd1-hooks.jsonl` to `cd7-hooks.jsonl`) and transcripts were.
- **Result:**
  - The countdown appeared only when the later script had started on the operator's Yes rather than on auto mode's own allowance. This matches a reading of the 2.1.328 binary made for the experiment (its output was not saved), not a documented rule: in the async `REPL` a running script keeps its classifier review until a newer script that auto mode did not allow starts, and after that each of its calls that would ask goes to the prompt. In the attempt with the `allow` hook the transcript still recorded the second script's acceptance as `source: "config", reasonType: "classifier"`, so it shows only that a script auto mode allowed left the earlier script's review in place, not what a hook's `allow` does.
  - In the attempt that left the first code running past its turn, each `Stop` carried `background_tasks: [{id, type: "code", status: "running", description}]`, and the code's `sleep 50` ended 48.7 s after the first `Stop`. Code can outlive its turn, unlike in M65's runs, and the `Stop` marker shows it.
  - The screen showed the ordinary Bash prompt with the countdown line, which counted down each second:

    ```
     Bash command
     Run shell command
     rm -f D:/git/switchboard/.wip/code-mode-exp/gates/logs/rm-me.txt
     ⚠ Claude Code will automatically block this request in 2:00, to avoid holding up an unattended session

     Do you want to proceed?
     ❯ 1. Yes
       2. No

     Esc to cancel · Tab to amend
    ```
    It offered no "don't ask again" answer. The script's earlier `Bash(rm …)` row showed `Running…` above it. The hook's own prompt for the second script had no countdown; it read `Hook PreToolUse:REPL requires confirmation for this tool: test ask [settings]` and `Yes runs Claude's code. …`, and fired `PermissionRequest` with `tool_name: "REPL"` and the code in `tool_input.code`.
  - Hooks: `PreToolUse` `Bash`, `PermissionRequest` with `tool_name: "Bash"`, `tool_input.command` and `permission_mode: "auto"`, then `Notification` `permission_prompt` (`Claude needs your permission`) 6.0 s later. No payload carries the countdown or its deadline. `claude agents --json` said `status: "waiting", waitingFor: "permission prompt"`. The turn was still open: the model was waiting for the first script.
  - At 120 s after the `PermissionRequest` the prompt closed and the call was refused with no `PermissionDenied`, `PostToolUse` or `PostToolUseFailure` for it. The code received `Permission denied for Bash: This Bash call was NOT run. Auto mode checks each piece of code as a whole before it starts, and that check does not cover this call. It needs the user's approval, and the user is not answering permission prompts at the moment. Do not get the same effect another way to avoid the approval. Go on with other work and tell the user which step was skipped and still needs their approval.`, and `$` threw `ShellError`. The script ended, then `PostToolUse` `REPL`, `PostToolBatch` and `Stop` (`background_tasks: []`) came within 2 s; the model reported the skipped step. The roster went back to `idle`.
- **Detection:** with Switchboard's matchers the wait is `PermissionRequest` (tool `Bash`) then `Notification` `permission_prompt`, which `markerState` reads as waiting on a permission prompt, as for any permission prompt (M65); the roster's `waitingFor` agrees. Only the screen tells the countdown apart, by the line `Claude Code will automatically block this request in M:SS`, which gives the time left. Expiry fires no hook of its own, so the marker stays waiting until the next `PostToolBatch` or `Stop`; here that was within 2 s, because the refusal ended the script.
- **Pass criteria (plan E3, countdown prompt):** the prompt is raised, and a held session waiting on it is detectable. **Passed:** raised and detected as a permission wait by markers and roster, with its countdown readable from the screen.
- **Not verified:** the docs' trigger with no hook, such as an `ask` permission rule or an unavailable classifier; answering Yes or No to the countdown; the docs' "later calls that would show a countdown are refused without a prompt" after an expiry; what the markers show when the refused script goes on making calls instead of ending; the IDE-connected case without a countdown.
- **Current use:** the countdown prompt reads as a permission wait, as any permission prompt does (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions).

## M81. node-pty and the built holder on Node.js 22 (E11)

- **Date:** 2026-11-19.
- **Versions:** Node.js 22.23.5 (`node-v22.23.5-win-x64.zip` from nodejs.org, SHA-256 checked, unpacked in `.wip/code-mode-exp/gates/node22`; ABI 127, N-API 10); Node.js 24.14.0 for comparison; node-pty 1.1.0; `packages/holder/dist` as built on 2026-11-18 (protocol 1); Windows 11 x64.
- **Experiment:** `e11-dist.mjs`, run on Node.js 22, started `packages/holder/dist/main.js` on the same Node.js, detached, with a scratch `--home`, connected with `HolderClient`, spawned `pwsh -Command` with a loop that printed `[Console]::WindowWidth` and `WindowHeight` at 80×24, attached with `Attachment.open`, resized to 132×40, listed, killed, and shut the holder down. The holder resolved node-pty from `packages/holder/node_modules`. Separately, the M76 probe VSIX (`run/probe.vsix`) was unzipped under the temp folder, away from any checkout, and a script on Node.js 22 loaded node-pty from its `extension/holder/node_modules` and spawned, resized and killed the same `pwsh` loop. The runs' output was read live and not saved.
- **Result:**
  - Built holder: it wrote its record and logged `Listening on 127.0.0.1:<port> for <home> (protocol 1, Node.js v22.23.5)`; the client's welcome had protocol 1. The child printed `PS 80x24`, and `PS 132x40` after the attached client's resize. `list` read the tree's memory (90 MB). `kill` returned exit code 1 after 1.6 s, and the pid was gone; `shutdown` logged `Exiting: shutdown was requested`.
  - Extracted VSIX: node-pty loaded from the extracted folder; the child printed `PS 80x24`, then `PS 132x40`; the kill ended it with exit code -1073741510 (`STATUS_CONTROL_C_EXIT`) and printed `Error: AttachConsole failed` from `conpty_console_list_agent.js` on stderr, as in M76.
  - A Node.js child (22 or 24) under ConPTY kept `process.stdout.columns` and `getWindowSize()` at 80×24 after the resize, both in the holder and with node-pty directly, under either Node.js version, although the resize redrew its output; a resize check needs a child that reads the console size, such as `pwsh`.
  - A holder whose test client exited before `shutdown` kept its home's lock, and the next holder on that home exited after its two-second wait for the lock, as `main.ts` intends; ending the old holder cleared it.
- **Pass criteria (plan E11, Node.js 22 on Windows x64):** spawn, resize and kill work under Node.js 22 from the built holder and from the extracted package. **Passed.** With M76 this covers Node.js 22 and 24 and Electron 43 on Windows x64.
- **Not verified:** a Code Mode `claude` held by a holder on Node.js 22; the hub started with the native binary missing; Linux x64 (plan Q8).
- **Current use:** the holder runs under `sessions.nodePath`, Node.js 22 or 24 (architecture.md § Terminal holder).

## M82. Memory of idle held Code Mode sessions over an hour

- **Date:** 2026-11-19.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.328 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; Node.js 24.14.0, node-pty 1.1.0, @xterm/headless 6.0.0, @xterm/addon-serialize 0.14.0; 617 to 717 processes on the machine during the hour.
- **Experiment:** the prototype holder (`.wip/code-mode-exp/holder.mjs`, 10,000 lines of scrollback per session) held three Code Mode sessions started as in M63 (`--permission-mode auto --model haiku`, `--settings` `env` with `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=https://api.anthropic.com` and the three credential variables empty) on a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` copied from the M70 one, whose `.claude.json` lists the operator's five user MCP servers, in a scratch git repository. Session 1 was given one turn that replied with the numbers 1 to 10,000, one per line, filling its headless buffer to 10,040 lines (10,000 of scrollback); sessions 2 and 3 got no turn. Neither `!cat` of a 9,800-line file (the shown output is cut to about 20 lines) nor a redirect to `/dev/tty` (the tool's shell has no terminal) put long output on the screen. Then a sampler ran every 60 s for 60 minutes (61 samples), each one `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` read in Windows PowerShell (M75's method, plus `PrivatePageCount` and the name), summing each session's tree with the holder's `treeOf` and reading the holder's own process; three more samples followed over the next 3 minutes. Nothing was attached and no input was sent during the hour. Raw samples: `.wip/code-mode-exp/e14-hour/samples.jsonl` (the hour) and `samples-after.jsonl` (the three after it); the screen, the kill times and the leftover-process check were read live and not saved.
- **Result:**

  MB, working set / private bytes, at the first and last of the 61 samples:

  | Process | Start | End (60 min) | Peak | Lowest | End − start |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Holder (node.exe alone, 3 sessions, one with 10,000 lines) | 137.7 / 120.1 | 67.2 / 120.1 | 138 / 120.2 | 66.9 / 120.1 | -70.5 / 0 |
  | Session 1 `claude.exe` (10,000-line turn) | 647 / 629.2 | 302.5 / 564.1 | 723.8 / 715.1 | 300.1 / 544 | -344.4 / -65.1 |
  | Session 1 tree (8 processes) | 951.8 / 823.4 | 479.1 / 758.5 | 1029.5 / 913.8 | 479.1 / 738.4 | -472.7 / -65.0 |
  | Session 2 `claude.exe` (idle) | 306.9 / 529.8 | 250.4 / 524.8 | 306.9 / 529.8 | 242.7 / 469.5 | -56.6 / -5 |
  | Session 2 tree (8 processes) | 612 / 730 | 429.5 / 719.6 | 612 / 730 | 422.2 / 664.3 | -182.5 / -10.4 |
  | Session 3 `claude.exe` (idle) | 304.5 / 533.9 | 247.8 / 502.7 | 304.5 / 533.9 | 240.2 / 467 | -56.7 / -31.2 |
  | Session 3 tree (8 processes) | 609.7 / 735.2 | 426.8 / 695.9 | 609.7 / 735.2 | 419.2 / 661.8 | -183.0 / -39.3 |

  - Each tree was `claude.exe` and the user MCP servers' `cmd.exe`, `conhost.exe` and three `node.exe` processes, about 305 MB working set and 200 MB private at the start, the same in every session; the member count stayed 8 for the hour.
  - Private bytes did not grow over the hour: the holder, each `claude.exe` and each tree ended at or below its start (one MCP `node.exe` ended 0.1 MB above). The holder's stayed between 120.1 and 120.2 MB. Idle `claude.exe` private bytes moved within 467 to 534 MB; they rose by small steps from about 482 MB at minute 20 to 503 and 493 MB in the samples after the hour. Session 1's peaked at 715 MB at minute 7 and settled between 544 and 584 MB.
  - Working sets fell in steps at minutes 36, 41, 46, 51 and 56 (holder 138 to 67 MB, idle `claude.exe` 277 to 243 MB, MCP servers 305 to about 178 MB per tree, session 1's `claude.exe` 559 to 302 MB) while private bytes moved by at most 13 MB, so Windows trimmed them; working set is not a measure of growth here.
  - A session wrote to its pseudo-terminal about 30 minutes 10 seconds after it started and every 30 minutes after that (13:24:56 and 13:54:56 for session 1, started 12:54:46; 13:28:08 and 13:58:08 for session 2 and 13:28:09 and 13:58:09 for session 3, started 12:57:58), and at no other time after its startup output; the visible screen did not change. Session 2's private bytes rose 7 and 29 MB at those writes, session 3's 4 and 2 MB.
  - The read: 668 to 1,118 ms (mean 826 ms) per process-table query, 0.4 to 1.9 ms to sum the three trees and the holder's; no read failed.
  - The holder's `/kill` (`taskkill /T /F`) ended each tree in 1.6 to 1.7 s, and `/shutdown` ended the holder; no process naming the scratch folder or the sessions was left.
- **Pass criteria (plan E14):** a method under 100 ms per list of 20 sessions; numbers for the idle limit, from an hour-long run. **Passed** on Windows: `list` answers from the sampled read (under 2 ms here for 3 trees, under 3 ms for 20 in M75), and an idle Code Mode session's tree ended the hour at about 430 to 480 MB working set and 690 to 760 MB private (420 to 610 MB and 660 to 735 MB for the never-used ones over the hour), of which the operator's MCP servers are about 180 MB and 200 MB, with no growth over an hour; the holder costs about 120 MB private with three sessions and 10,000 lines in one.
- **Not verified:** Linux `/proc`; more than an hour, where the small steps in idle private bytes at the 30-minute writes could add up; a session with 10,000 lines of scrollback in the real holder (`packages/holder`); what the 30-minute write is.
- **Current use:** the 1-hour idle end of held sessions (`CODE_MODE_IDLE_MS`) and the memory cost it names (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, End rule).

## M83. File links in a Code Mode session's terminal

- **Date:** 2026-11-19.
- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.328 in Code Mode, with `PANEL_PROMPT` copied from the VS Code extension 2.1.327; VS Code's integrated terminal and Windows Terminal on Windows 11.
- **Experiment:** operator-observed in live use: a Code Mode session following the panel's "Code References in Text" section, which gives workspace-relative targets such as `src/filename.ts`, wrote Markdown file links, and the operator tried to open them from each terminal.
- **Result:** both terminals rendered a link as its text followed by its target in parentheses, as `README.md (src/README.md)`. VS Code's terminal made the path clickable (Ctrl+click) only when it started with `./` or was a `file://` URI; `src/README.md` was not.
- **Not verified:** Windows Terminal with a `./` target; a Linux terminal; whether a session follows the `./` instruction in every reply.
- **Current use:** the "Links in this terminal" section (`LINK_PROMPT`) of every Code Mode start and resume (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Command).

## M84. Live acceptance of Code Mode sessions, 2026-11-19

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.328, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; VS Code 1.146.0 with the Claude Code extension 2.1.328, which Code Mode sessions do not use; Windows Terminal 1.24.12741.0; Node.js 24.14.0.
- **Setup:** the operator ran it by hand on their own installed extension and dock, not an isolated acceptance home: the VSIX with Code Mode sessions installed (hub restarted at 10:27 local), the dock published at 10:47, and `sessions.codeMode` on in their Switchboard `settings.json`, with `sessions.installHooks` on and the hub's default 4-hour idle limit, since replaced by a fixed 1-hour limit (M82). Each result below is what the operator saw.
- **Start: pass.** An ad-hoc Claude session started in Code Mode in the terminal holder; Open in terminal showed Code Mode's `is on` line (M63).
- **Gateway: pass.** The session's `/status` showed the direct Anthropic API and the subscription sign-in, not the operator's local gateway (M64).
- **Attach in Windows Terminal: pass.** Closing the terminal tab and opening it again replayed the screen as Claude drew it; typing, Shift+Tab and Esc reached Claude.
- **Attach in VS Code's terminal: pass.** Open in terminal from VS Code replayed the screen, and keys reached Claude.
- **Question: pass.** A session's `AskUserQuestion` was answered in its attached terminal; the dock row showed it waiting, then working.
- **Stop: pass.** Stop on an idle session ended it; its terminal offered to resume, and Enter resumed the same conversation.
- **Held sessions: pass.** The dock's Held Sessions window and `hub.js holder list` listed the session and could stop it.
- **Hub restart: pass.** A VS Code window reload, which restarted the hub, during a turn running `sleep 60` left the session running, and it reported done.
- **Holder loss: pass.** Killing the holder process with `Stop-Process -Force` during a turn made the work item blocked with "The terminal holder stopped."; Open in terminal resumed the session in a new holder.
- **Delete: pass.** Deleting the work item while its terminal was attached ended the session.
- **File links:** how the attached terminals show a session's Markdown file links is recorded in M83.
- **Pass criteria (plan E10, eye check):** the replay shows the screen as Claude drew it, and keys reach Claude, in Windows Terminal and in VS Code's terminal. **Passed** for the replay, typing, Shift+Tab and Esc; paste and Ctrl+O rest on M72's raw-mode client.
- **Not covered:** the idle limit, tested only in Vitest, which needs a hub started by hand with `--code-mode-idle-ms`; an orphan in the Held Sessions window and `hub.js holder list`; answering a question once in the item window and once in the terminal app; paste and Ctrl+O in either terminal; Claude runs nested in a session Switchboard started (M77); Linux. Steps 1–4 were not run step by step: ordinary background Claude and Codex sessions kept running unchanged during the run. M63, M65, M70 and M71 were not re-run as experiments on 2.1.328.

## M85. The read-only conversation of a Code Mode session, 2026-11-19

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.328, Haiku, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; Node.js 24.14.0; VS Code 1.146.0.
- **Setup:** the built hub bundle on Node.js under an isolated home in the worktree's `.wip/live`, with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `CODEX_HOME` there too, so its hooks and its background service stayed out of the operator's: a copy of the operator's `.credentials.json` and `.claude.json`, deleted afterwards, with the scratch repository trusted. `sessions.codeMode`, `installHooks` and `concurrency: 4`. A driver started four ad-hoc sessions in a scratch git repository: one answered a prompt, three ran a PowerShell loop printing a line every 2 seconds for 90 seconds. It connected as the first session's item window (`/events?workspace=<its workspace file>`) and as another session's, opened the first window's conversation stream, attached to the session through the holder and typed three messages into its terminal, as the operator would, and polled the transcripts every 20 ms to time each line against the event it became.
- **Start prompt:** a scratch repository inside the worktree made each session ask "Allow external CLAUDE.md file imports?", because the worktree's `CLAUDE.md` imports `AGENTS.md` from outside the session's folder; the start check read it as a start prompt (M70), and Enter on the default, No, started the turn.
- **Result:** the window's `hello` named its work item and current session, with `rules: current`; its `state` held its own work item only, with `needsCount`. `history` with a forged session ID, and with this session's ID through the other window's key, answered 403 "Not in this window.". Each typed message and each reply arrived as an event; the reply asked for a list came with its list after `[details]`, and "Actually I meant Italy, not France." got a `[rewrite]` that marked the French reply rewritten. A reply written as text before a 6-second tool call came first from the screen (published when the 300 ms hold ran out) and was replaced in place by its transcript copy 0.6 seconds later; no screen copy was withdrawn. With the three other sessions working, a transcript line reached the window's stream in 50 to 254 ms over 11 lines (median 128 ms), within the 500 ms limit; the hub logged no snapshot worker failure.
- **Not covered:** a 95th percentile over many lines, the Conversation view itself, which the operator checks in VS Code, and a session in the full-screen renderer, which Switchboard now never starts (`tui: "default"`).
- **Secondary side bar (spike):** the integration suite, on VS Code 1.147.0 (the build `@vscode/test-cli` downloaded), accepted the `viewsContainers.secondarySidebar` contribution, registered `workbench.view.extension.switchboard-conversation` and `switchboard.conversation.focus`, resolved the view in the secondary side bar and fed it its stream's `hello`. On the operator's VS Code 1.146.0, Claude Code 2.1.328 contributes its own panel the same way and sets its activity bar fallback only below 1.106 (its `extension.js`), the rule Switchboard copies; the view was not opened in 1.146.0 itself.

## M86. Typing a message into a held Code Mode session, 2026-11-19

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.328, Haiku, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; Node.js 24.14.0.
- **Setup:** as M85: the built hub bundle under an isolated home in the worktree's `.wip/live2`, with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `CODEX_HOME` there, a copy of the operator's `.credentials.json` and `.claude.json` with a scratch repository outside the worktree trusted, deleted afterwards with Claude's `.claude.json` backups; `sessions.codeMode` on, model `haiku`. One ad-hoc session; its start asked "Allow external CLAUDE.md file imports?" for the operator's `~/.claude/AGENTS.md`, answered No with Enter through the holder. A driver connected as the session's item window, opened its conversation stream and sent messages with `POST /v1/conversation/send`.
- **Result:**
  - A one-line message was pasted, its Enter taken first time, and `sent` 0.49 s after the command, confirmed by the transcript; the reply answered it. A message beginning `/help` went escaped as `\\/help …`, opened no command, and was confirmed. A message sent while the session's turn ran was confirmed by its `enqueue` queue operation and answered in that turn. A 2,070-character line was confirmed. With a key typed and erased in an attached terminal 50 ms before the send, the message went 2.1 s after the last key, after the 1.5-second pause. A repeated `opId` answered the first message; a stale `scopeGen` got 409 and a forged session ID 403.
  - **A paste of several lines** is recorded in the transcript's user entry, and in its queue operation, wrapped in tags on lines of their own: `<pasted_content id="914d">\n<the text>\n</pasted_content id="914d">`, with a random id. Before the reader removed them, the message was not confirmed and the transcript line showed as a second message with the tags; with `unwrapPasted` the message was confirmed in place. The screen showed the text in the input box while it waited, so the draft guard and the Enter retry hold for it.
- **Not covered:** an Enter dropped by Claude and retried, a menu open while a message waits, and a hub restart while a message is typed, which the Vitest suites cover with fakes; the Conversation view's composer itself, which the operator checks in VS Code.
- **Current use:** typing from the Conversation view, and `unwrapPasted` in its confirmation (architecture.md § Typing a message).

## M87. A prompt-less Code Mode start, 2026-11-19

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.329, Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; Node.js 24.14.0, node-pty 1.1.0.
- **Setup:** a driver spawned `claude` in a node-pty pseudo-terminal of 120×40 with `codeModeStartArgs` and `codeModeEnvironment` from the built `packages/claude`, the final prompt argument left out, a contract from `sessionContract` with the reports `stage`, `worktree` and `link` and the line "Code word one is OSPREY.", and the conversation rules, under a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` holding a copy of the operator's `.credentials.json` and `.claude.json` (deleted afterwards) and a `settings.json` whose hooks logged every event with `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH`; a `SessionStart` `resume` hook returned `additionalContext` "Code word two is HERON.", standing in for the contract hook. Scratch git repositories outside any checkout, one trusted in the copied `.claude.json` and one not.
- **Result:**
  - **Idle start.** The `is on` line was on screen 1.6 s after the spawn, with the input box and its suggested prompt; the only hook in the next 15 s was `SessionStart` `startup`, and `claude agents --json` listed the process with `status: "idle"`. No transcript file existed yet.
  - **First reply.** A message pasted and submitted gave `UserPromptSubmit` and `Stop`; the reply named code word one and the report commands `stage`, `worktree`, `link` and `handled`, so the contract and rules in `--append-system-prompt` reached the model without a first prompt.
  - **Resume.** After `taskkill /T /F` of that process, `codeModeResumeArgs` with a new launch showed the `is on` line and gave only `SessionStart` `resume` (idle); the next message's reply named both code words, the second from the hook's `additionalContext`.
  - **Resume before any message.** A prompt-less session killed before its first message has no transcript; `claude --resume` printed `No conversation found with session ID: <id>` and exited, with `SessionEnd` as its only hook.
  - **Trust and other dialogs.** In the untrusted folder the folder trust dialog ("❯ No, exit" selected) came first and no hook fired; Down and Enter (Yes) led to "Allow external CLAUDE.md file imports?" for the operator's `~/.claude/AGENTS.md`, still with no hook, as in M70. Enter on the default "No, exit" printed "Claude Code didn't start because this folder isn't trusted." and exited.
- **Pass criteria (slice 6a spike):** a prompt-less start comes up idle with Code Mode on; the contract and rules reach the first reply; a resume is idle and gets the contract hook's context; the trust prompt is told from a start. **Passed**, with one limit: a session with no message cannot be resumed.
- **Not verified:** Linux; an automatic compaction of a prompt-less session.
- **Current use:** the prompt-less hosted start (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Liveness and state and Start check; § Hosted conversations), including the restart, under its session ID and with no prompt, of a session that ended before its first message.

## M88. Mentions, slash commands and images pasted into a Code Mode session, 2026-11-19

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.329, Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; Node.js 24.14.0.
- **Setup:** `claude` with `--settings {"viewMode":"focus","tui":"default"}`, `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE=1` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` in the worktree's `.wip/live7`, holding a copy of the operator's `.credentials.json` and parts of `.claude.json` with a scratch repository outside the worktree trusted, all deleted afterwards; run in a node-pty pseudo-terminal by a driver that wrote bracketed pastes and Enter as separate writes, as the holder's `write` does, read the screen through a headless xterm, and read the transcript. The repository held `notes.txt`, `dir with space/x.txt`, a PNG with the text "ZEBRA 42" and a project skill `hello7`.
- **Result:**
  - **Mentions.** "Summarize @notes.txt in one short line." pasted mid-text added a `file` attachment with the file's text after the user line, and the reply quoted it. `@"dir with space/x.txt" what is the code?`, quoted and first in the paste, attached the file. `\@notes.txt what is the secret word?`, as `pasteText` escaped a first `@` until then, attached nothing. An absolute mention outside the session's folder, `@c:/…/outside7.png`, attached the image.
  - **Images.** `What text is in the image @shot.png ?` added a `file` attachment of `type: "image"` with the PNG in base64, and the reply read "ZEBRA 42". A paste of an image file's absolute path alone showed `[Image #1]` in the input box. Alt+V (`ESC v`) written to the terminal read the operator's clipboard image and showed `[Image #2]`; Ctrl+V (`0x16`) did nothing. Neither placeholder was sent.
  - **Slash commands.** `/hello7` pasted unescaped and followed by Enter ran the skill: the transcript recorded a user line `<command-message>hello7</command-message>\n<command-name>/hello7</command-name>`, with no `<command-args>` line, then the skill's text as a meta line, and the reply. `/clear` ran too and started a new transcript file, whose first lines are a `<local-command-caveat>` line and the `/clear` line in its tags with an empty `<command-args>`.
  - **Skill listing.** The first message's lines included a `skill_listing` attachment, `{type, content, skillCount, isInitial, names}` (some sessions also `digests`), whose `names` held the project skill `hello7` first, the user's and plugins' skills (`anthropic-skills:docx`) and built-in prompt commands such as `init` and `security-review`, and whose `content` has one `- name: description` line each. The operator's own transcripts show later listings with `isInitial: false` that add a skill or change a description. Built-in local commands such as `/clear` and `/compact` are not listed.
- **Not covered:** a mention or command typed through the hub's `send` and the holder, which the Vitest suites cover with fakes; the Conversation view's composer itself, which the operator checks in VS Code; an image larger than the API's limits.
- **Current use:** typing a mention or a slash command and confirming it (`pasteText`, `typedText`, architecture.md § Typing a message), the composer's slash command list (`skill_listing`, architecture.md § Slash commands), and pasted images sent as a file mention rather than through the terminal's clipboard key.

## M89. Extension terminals across a window reload, an extension host restart and a quit, 2026-11-19

- **Historical:** the VS Code extension and its terminals were removed on 2026-11-24; Open in terminal now always opens the terminal app, and the web page's Console tab attaches in the page.
- **Versions:** VS Code 1.146.0 on the operator's machine; source read from `microsoft/vscode` `main` on 2026-11-19 (`extHostTerminalService.ts`, `mainThreadTerminalService.ts`, `terminalService.ts`, `terminalInstance.ts`, `terminalProcessManager.ts`, `ptyService.ts`, `terminalConfiguration.ts`).
- **Experiment:** source inspection only; no window was reloaded. The real-host suite (VS Code 1.147.0) ran Open in terminal against stand-in terminals that report a process ID.
- **Result:**
  - A terminal created with `createTerminal({shellPath, shellArgs, env})` persists by default (`terminal.integrated.enablePersistentSessions` true, no `isTransient`). On a window reload the pty host keeps its process, and the workbench recreates the terminal with `shellLaunchConfig: {attachPersistentProcess}` and attaches to the same process, with the same process ID.
  - A new extension host learns of every open terminal through `$acceptTerminalOpened`, which builds `creationOptions` from the instance's `shellLaunchConfig`. For a reconnected terminal that holds only `attachPersistentProcess`, so `creationOptions.shellPath`, `shellArgs`, `env` and `name` are undefined: neither the command nor an environment variable set at creation identifies it. After a restart of the extension host alone, the instance keeps its original `shellLaunchConfig`, so `creationOptions` still match. `terminal.processId` resolves to the process ID in both cases.
  - The terminal's `name` is its title, which a title escape sequence from the process can change (`_setTitle` ignores only process-name titles for a terminal named through the API), so it does not identify a session.
  - After VS Code quits (`terminal.integrated.persistentSessionReviveProcess`, default `onExit`), the pty host revives the terminal on next start: `_reviveTerminalProcess` runs the saved `shellLaunchConfig`, the same executable and arguments, as a new process with the old buffer replayed, under a new process ID unless the operating system happens to reuse the old one.
- **Not verified:** a live reload; how long a reconnected terminal takes to report its process ID (a lookup waits 1 s for each, then opens a new terminal); whether a revived `claude attach <short ID>` still attaches (the short ID can be stale) and whether a revived `claude --resume` starts a copy of a session that is live elsewhere (M18).
- **Used until 2026-11-24:** Open in terminal's reuse of a terminal by its kept process ID (`TerminalKeys`, architecture.md § Claude Code runner). A revived terminal runs a new process, so it is not reused; a process ID the operating system reuses after a quit can be taken by chance.

## M90. Clearing Claude Code's input box, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.329 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; Node.js 24.14.0, node-pty and @xterm/headless as in M63.
- **Setup:** a driver (`.wip/live-clear/run2.mjs` and `run3.mjs` in the worktree) spawned `claude --model haiku --settings <tui default and the M64 env>` in a node-pty pseudo-terminal of 120×40 in a scratch git repository outside the worktree, with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` at a scratch folder holding copies of the operator's `.credentials.json` and `.claude.json` (the repository marked trusted), deleted afterwards; no hooks, no user settings. Each step wrote bytes as the holder does and read the input box with `readInputScreen`.
- **Keys:** the default keybindings in the binary map `ctrl+l` to `chat:clearInput` and `chat:clearScreen`, one handler, `escape` to `chat:cancel`, and Esc over typed text shows "Esc again to clear".
- **Result:**

  | Input box | Key | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | one pasted line | Ctrl+L (`\x0c`) | unchanged: it redraws the screen and leaves the text |
  | one pasted line | Ctrl+U (`\x15`) | empty |
  | three pasted lines, cursor at the end | Ctrl+U five times | each press deletes the text before the cursor on its line, or at a line's start the line break before it: `one/two/three` → `one/two/` → `one/two` → `one/` → `one` → empty |
  | empty | Ctrl+U | empty; no menu, no hint, no exit |
  | one pasted line | Esc, Esc | the first shows "Esc again to clear", the second empties it; on an empty box a second Esc opens Rewind, and Esc during a turn is `chat:cancel` |
  | a 1,600-character line, shown as `[Pasted text #1]` | Ctrl+U | empty in one press |
  | 30 lines, shown as `[Pasted text #2 +29 lines]` | Ctrl+U | empty in one press |
  | one line pasted while a turn ran `sleep 15` in the shell | Ctrl+U | empty; the turn went on and replied, with no "Interrupted" |

  Ctrl+U shows "ctrl+y to paste deleted text" under the box; the text is not sent.
- **Pass criteria:** a key that empties the box, never submits, interrupts a turn or opens a menu, also on an empty box. **Passed by Ctrl+U**; Ctrl+L does not clear, and Esc can open Rewind or cancel a turn.
- **Not verified:** a user keybinding that rebinds `ctrl+u` (`keybindings.json`); the classic renderer only.
- **Current use:** clearing a typed message the transcript does not show with Ctrl+U (`CLEAR_LINE`, architecture.md § Typing a message).

## M91. Speech out: realtime speech and decibri playback, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300, Node.js 24.14.0 (`sessions.nodePath`'s `node`), decibri 5.8.0 with `@decibri/decibri-win32-x64-msvc`, OpenAI `gpt-realtime-2.1-mini` through `wss://api.openai.com/v1/realtime`, the key read as claude-collab's `tts.js` reads it.
- **Setup:** scripts in the session scratch folder drove the built `packages/voice` modules: `Tts` alone, writing its PCM to a WAV file; `VoiceService` with the real `Tts` and a recording playback that plays nothing, given targets and the focus, then a `speak` and a second reply of the same turn, then a focus move; and decibri's `Speaker` alone, opened at 24 kHz mono and given 100 ms of silence. Nothing was heard. The packaged `voice.js`, extracted from a built VSIX, started in a temporary home in the extension's test suite.
- **Result:** a passage of 61 characters on a cold connection gave its first audio after 1.73 s and 3.9 s of audio in 2.06 s; with the connection opened when the target was set (`warm`), a passage of 83 characters gave its first audio 1.29 s after `speak`. The focus move dropped the target at once (generation 2) and stopped the playback in the same tick, and the debounce set the new target 500 ms later (generation 3). decibri listed one output device, opened the default one and played the silence; the packaged `voice.js` loaded decibri from its own `node_modules` (the binary needs `onnxruntime.dll` beside it), wrote `voice.json`, refused a wrong token, greeted the hub with `active` and `audio` (`engine: decibri`, no error) and exited on `shutdown`, removing its record.
- **Not verified:** audible playback, an output device chosen by name, playback while another app plays, the focus-to-playback-stopped limit under snapshot load through the hub (architecture.md § Voice), Linux.
- **Current use:** the voice process's speech and playback (architecture.md § Voice).

## M92. Codex daemon, native conversations and recovery, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Codex CLI and managed app-server 0.170.0, Windows 11 10.0.26300, Node.js 24.14.0; generated protocol types from that CLI. VS Code real-host checks use the integration suite's isolated installation.
- **Setup:** scratch repositories and separate Codex homes under `.wip/codex-compat-assessment`, with temporary copies of the sign-in file, removed after each run. No operator daemon was stopped or changed. `daemon-probe.mjs`, `native-probe.mjs`, `hub-live.mjs`, `options-live.mjs`, `compaction-live.mjs` and `runner-compaction-live.mjs` retain the drivers and local evidence; recorded test fixtures contain scrubbed native items and a question only.
- **Daemon and transport: pass.** `codex app-server daemon start` starts a managed daemon on Windows, with a control socket under `<CODEX_HOME>/app-server-control/`. `app-server proxy` is a raw byte relay, not a JSONL client: newline-delimited JSON alone gets no initialize response. WebSocket framing over the proxy's stdio duplex stream works. A WebSocket write callback can report success as `null`; treating only `undefined` as success fails initialization.
- **Turn survival and history: pass.** A thread ran a 12-second PowerShell sleep. Killing the proxy left the turn active; a new proxy rejoined it, added a steering message and received the final reply containing both requested code words. Native history returned user items with `clientId` matching `clientUserMessageId`, completed reply items, tool items and turn state. New threads used paginated history, with no legacy rollout file required.
- **Pending question and terminal: pass.** A plan-mode turn called `request_user_input`. Rejoining after disconnect replayed the same pending request, including native request ID 0. An API response with `answers: {<question id>: {answers: [<text>]}}` completed it. While the API client remained connected, `codex resume --remote unix://D:/<isolated home>/app-server-control/app-server-control.sock <thread>` in a pseudo-terminal displayed the same pending question. The harness needed `TERM=xterm-256color`; inherited `dumb` showed a terminal confirmation first.
