ConPTY harness cleanup printed `AttachConsole failed`; the API subsequently answered the question successfully. This verifies captured terminal output, not an eye check in the operator's terminal app.
- **Built hub acceptance: pass.** Through `createHubApp` and the real loopback server under isolated settings, a hosted Codex conversation started without Claude Code Mode or hooks, accepted a message through `/v1/conversation/send` and emitted its reply. Restarting the hub during a 15-second tool call preserved the same thread and replayed the reply without duplicate message identities. The session wrote an artifact, whose change reached the conversation stream, and its report CLI set its stage. Stopping the first thread left a second thread running through its final reply and left the daemon running. Evidence: `.wip/codex-compat-assessment/acceptance/result.json` and `hub.log`.
- **Instruction refresh: limits and verified alternative.** `thread/resume` with changed `developerInstructions` on an already loaded thread left the original code word in force. `thread/inject_items` with a developer message changed the next reply to the new code word. Compaction sometimes dropped the injected update; that failing run was observed in this session, but its scratch log was overwritten. Reinjecting after a completed `contextCompaction` item restored it. A deliberately low automatic compaction threshold produced 17 compactions, and each subsequent reply kept the updated code word with event-driven reinjection. The deprecated `thread/compacted` notification was not emitted. Evidence: `.wip/codex-compat-assessment/compaction2/result.json` and `.wip/codex-compat-assessment/compaction2-live.log`.
- **Environment, images and debriefs: pass.** A per-thread `config` override of `shell_environment_policy.set.CLAUDE_ARTIFACTS_DIR` reached the PowerShell tool. A local image input was recorded in the user message. An ephemeral read-only app-server fork returned its debrief answer while the parent's turn count stayed unchanged. Evidence: `.wip/codex-compat-assessment/options/result.json`.
- **Built runner compaction recovery: pass.** The built runner restored the updated code word after a live compaction. After its client closed, another client compacted the thread; a new runner recovered from history and the next reply kept the update. Evidence: `.wip/codex-compat-assessment/rc/result.json` and `.wip/codex-compat-assessment/runner-compaction-live.log`.
- **Limits:** Linux was not run live. Native command/file approval responses, unknown-delivery recovery, optional skill lookup failures, release races and stale scope/request refusals are covered by injected-protocol tests; the live native-request experiment used a question. Speech and persona refresh use the shared machinery and the verified developer-message mechanism; no new audible playback check was run. Native control-socket path limits apply to `CODEX_HOME`, including on Windows: an overly long scratch path failed daemon startup with `path must be shorter than SUN_LEN`; shortening the isolated home resolved it.
- **Current use:** PRD R107 and the Codex app-server runner, including its instruction injection after a change or a compaction (architecture.md § Codex runner, Input and recovery). Re-run these mechanisms after upgrading Codex.

## M93. Hosted conversation dock state and folder deployment, 2026-11-20

- **Historical:** the dock and its folder-window switching were removed on 2026-11-24, with the VS Code extension; the row marks still hold, and the web page's Open in VS Code opens a folder-hosted conversation's folder.
- **Versions:** Codex CLI 0.170.0, VS Code 1.146.0 (`69047191fc427f2f92828279aae1d90d8eeda7ab`), Switchboard VSIX 0.0.0 with hub build `d70cb548c87f6d78`. The old dock binary was dated 2026-11-19 20:45, before folder support in `8f5b910`; the replacement was published from `159b184`, with no dock source changes in this fix.
- **Evidence:** the operator's AdHoc 22 had an idle Codex app-server session and a `running` work item. The hub's dock stream marked its row Running and on the agent's turn. See architecture.md § Rows for the distinction between an open conversation and a working session.
- **Folder click:** the hub sent `file: null`, `hosted: true` and the correct canonical folder. The installed dock predated folder-window switching and its assembly lacked `FolderWindow`. Publishing the current dock and restarting it resolved the mismatch. A forwarded `/dock/click` for AdHoc 22 left its session for the folder and that folder window claimed a `conversation` command. The Conversation view's accessibility tree showed the expected conversation and Idle.
- **Limits:** the dock's tool window was not exposed by the desktop automation inventory, so this was a forwarded click, not a pointer click on its row. The screenshot helper captured another foreground window; the view check rests on its accessibility tree. The first integration run passed 106 tests and failed its theme-focus assertion; the rerun passed all 107 with no desktop automation or dock restart during the run.
- **Current use:** PRD R27, R102 and R104: the row marks (architecture.md § Rows).

## M94. Dictation: capture, other apps' recording, recognition and turn detection, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300 over Remote Desktop (default capture device "Microphone"), Node.js 24.14.0, decibri 5.8.0, sherpa-onnx-node 2.0.1 (`sherpa-onnx-win-x64`, CPU), NVIDIA Nemotron Speech Streaming 0.6B 160 ms int8 (`sherpa-onnx-nemotron-speech-streaming-en-0.6b-160ms-int8-2026-04-25`), onnxruntime-node 1.31.1, `@huggingface/tokenizers` 0.2.0, LiveKit's turn detector (`model_q8.onnx`, English threshold 0.011), Windows PowerShell 5.1.
- **Setup:** scripts in the session scratch folder, played nothing aloud: decibri's `Microphone` at 16 kHz with `aec: {model: 'tau', referenceSampleRate: 24000}` for 2.5 s; `micuse.ps1` run by a harness while a second Node.js process captured for 2.5 s; the recogniser on four of claude-collab's saved 8 s clips; the turn model on four short chats; and the built `voice.js` and `turn.js` from `extensions/switchboard/dist/voice` in a temporary home holding copies of the two models, driven as the hub drives it.
- **Reference timing:** the operator's first run of the echo check over Remote Desktop pushed the reference as each 100 ms was written to the speaker and cancelled nothing: `erleDb` 0, 0.0 dB level reduction, `referenceDropped` about 31,000, `acquisitionParked` 41,771 and 132,099, `delaySamples` 7198 then null. A `Speaker` at 24 kHz took about 33 writes of 100 ms of silence within 12 ms, then one every 100 ms, and drained at 8.0 s for 8.0 s written: its queue holds about 32 writes, so with 100 ms writes `writeAsync` resolves about 3.3 s before the audio plays. decibri 5.8.0's changelog says the reference queue holds 2 s, so the reference pushed past that was dropped, which `referenceDropped` shows; it also says a reference pushed far ahead of its echo leaves the delay unacquired, which may explain the rest but was not shown apart. Dictation on local speakers, not over Remote Desktop, also heard the voice's own spoken replies, fed the same way. The voice now pushes the reference as it plays (architecture.md § Dictation). The same run opened two `Microphone` streams on the one device, raw and cancelled, and neither recognised the operator's sentence; the check now opens one at a time.
- **Second run, local speakers:** with the reference pushed as it played, `referenceDropped` was 0 and the reference ran at most 20 ms ahead, but the canceller still removed nothing (0.1 dB, 26 of 27 played words left; `erleDb` 0, `doubleTalk` true at the end, `delaySamples` about 6700, so a delay was acquired). In decibri-aec 0.2.0's `tau`, `doubleTalk` reports a coherence collapse or output protection, so it does not by itself show a talker. Offline analysis of the saved captures: the echo arrived about 130 to 250 ms after each write, about 13 dB below the clip; an adaptive 200 ms NLMS filter reached 6.5 dB on the raw capture, and a 200 ms filter trained on the first playback and then frozen reached about 9.5 dB on the next two, so a linear filter does remove some echo, and why `tau` removed none is not established. The raw silent run's playbacks kept their spacing within 0.13 ms, so clock drift is not shown. Asked through `IAudioEffectsManager`, Windows reported for the default capture device (Logitech BRIO, played through LG HDR 4K over NVIDIA HDMI audio): in the default mode decibri uses, noise suppression on and echo cancellation off; in communications mode (`AudioCategory_Communications`), echo cancellation, noise suppression and gain control on, with `IAcousticEchoCancellationControl` accepting the default speakers as its reference. Noise suppression ahead of the canceller may contribute; that was not tested apart. A communications-mode capture (`packages/voice/assets/commcapture.ps1`) delivered 1.48 s of 16 kHz mono PCM in 1.5 s with no discontinuity; its device position counts the device's 48 kHz frames, not the converted ones.
- **Result:** capture delivered 24 chunks of 3200 bytes in 2.4 s; with no reference pushed the canceller searched (`delaySamples` null, `acquisitionParked` counting) and passed the audio through. The watcher printed `{"busy":false}` 0.35 s after it started, `{"busy":true,"apps":["node"]}` 30 ms after the other process opened the device and `false` within 10 ms of its `stop()`, and exited when its stdin closed. The recogniser loaded in 1.9 s and decoded each 8 s clip in about 2.1 s, matching claude-collab's transcripts. The turn model loaded in 1.0 s and answered in 21 to 27 ms: "use 8799" after "Which port should the hub use, 8765 or 8799?" scored 0.62, "I think we should use the" 0.0004. The built voice process went `starting` to `listening` 2.7 s after `mic` (the model load), to `typing` and back on `typing`, and `off` on `mic {on: false}`; two identical heard tags promoted the term and the next recogniser's hotwords file held it. onnxruntime-common's CommonJS build needs its `dist/cjs/package.json` (`"type": "commonjs"`), which the voice build copies.
- **Third run, Windows' echo cancellation:** `packages/voice/scripts/echo-check.mjs` (a TTS clip played aloud three times per run, four runs with one mic stream each: raw and cancelled, silent and talking; each capture saved as a WAV and recognised) passed on the same machine with the cancelled runs through `commcapture.ps1` in communications mode, echo cancellation on and the speakers as reference: run 2 heard 0 of the 8 played words at a level of 0.0000, 52.6 dB below the raw run, and run 3 kept all 5 of the operator's words read aloud while the clip played. With the helper running as a child of a Node.js process, `micuse.ps1` given that process's PID reported no other app, and given 0 reported `powershell` while the helper recorded.
- **Not verified:** barge-in (M96) over Windows' echo cancellation, whose gain control and noise suppression also change the level and sound the voice check reads, so a voiceprint enrolled through decibri's capture may need enrolling again; Remote Desktop's "Remote Audio" device in communications mode, anything dictated aloud, Teams or Zoom holding the mic while muted, a Remote Desktop reconnect recreating the capture device, a capture device chosen by name, the GPU build, and Linux (`pactl` against PipeWire).
- **Current use:** dictation capture (architecture.md § Dictation).

## M95. Resuming a stopped Codex app-server thread, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Codex CLI 0.171.0, Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300, Node.js 24.14.0; protocol schema from `codex app-server generate-json-schema` of that CLI.
- **Setup:** a scratch `CODEX_HOME` with no sign-in and `codex app-server --listen stdio://` driven by a probe script in the session scratch folder; no daemon was started, and the operator's Codex home and daemon were not touched. No turn ran, since the home had no sign-in.
- **Protocol:** `thread/resume` takes `threadId` and optional `cwd`, `model`, `approvalPolicy`, `approvalsReviewer`, `sandbox`, `config`, `developerInstructions` and `excludeTurns`; it loads a thread that is not loaded from its rollout, and rejoins one that is.
- **Result:** a thread started read-only with developer instructions `DEV-A`, given an injected developer message `INJ-1`, then unsubscribed by its only client stayed loaded and `idle` in that process 8 seconds later (`thread/loaded/list` listed it) while a `thread/status/changed` said `notLoaded`. Resuming it with `developerInstructions: "DEV-B"`, `sandbox: "workspace-write"` and `approvalPolicy: "on-request"` answered the approval policy asked for and the read-only sandbox it started with. A new app-server process read it as `notLoaded`; resuming it there with `sandbox: "workspace-write"`, `approvalPolicy: "never"` and `developerInstructions: "DEV-C"` loaded it `idle` with `never` and still read-only. The rollout held `DEV-A` and `INJ-1` and never `DEV-C`.
- **Not verified:** a model turn after either resume, so whether `DEV-B` or `DEV-C` reached the model; a resume through the daemon's control socket; Linux.
- **Current use:** resuming an ended app-server session (architecture.md § Codex runner, Resume; PRD R107).

## M96. Voice check and barge-in on saved clips, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300, Node.js 24.14.0, sherpa-onnx-node 2.0.1 (`sherpa-onnx-win-x64`, CPU), WeSpeaker ResNet34 (`wespeaker_en_voxceleb_resnet34_LM.onnx` from claude-collab's `.wip/models`).
- **Setup:** `.wip/slice14-live.mjs` in the slice 14 worktree loaded the built `voiceprint.js` and `barge.js` with the model copied into a scratch home, enrolled from claude-collab's 60 s `.wip/audio/enroll.wav`, checked the last 300 of its saved clips with their recorded scores, and played 20 of the operator's clips (median pitch under 150 Hz, score over 0.8, 3 s or longer) and its 6 clips with a median over 230 Hz through `BargeIn` in 160 ms chunks over the `marin` reply's audio 20 dB under each clip, after learning `marin`'s print from 4 windows of it, as claude-collab's `test-barge.js` B and C do, with no words heard. Nothing was played aloud.
- **Result:** the model loaded in 0.15 s; enrolment took 1.9 s for 19 windows, and its print had a similarity of 1 with claude-collab's `voiceprint.json`, with the same pitch range (median 113 Hz, p90 131 Hz). Every clip scored as claude-collab had recorded (median and p90 difference 0); the check took 140 to 320 ms per piece of 4 to 8 s, most of it the pitch estimate. Barge-in fired on all 20 of the operator's clips, a median 2.1 s into each clip (leading silence included), and not on the echo alone; of the 6 high clips, the 3 with 40 or more voiced frames (246 to 340 Hz, other voices) did not fire, and 3 with 4 to 11 voiced frames, short phrases whose median reads high from too few frames, did. A check of a loud 1.2 s window took 56 ms.
- **Not verified:** anything said aloud, enrolment through the dock and the voice process's capture, barge-in against decibri's echo canceller on speakers or over Remote Desktop, the operator's own voice against other people in the room, and Linux.
- **Current use:** the voice check and barge-in (architecture.md § Voice check and barge-in).

## M97. Speech recognition on the GPU, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300, Node.js 24.14.0, NVIDIA RTX 2000 Ada Generation (16 GB) with driver 596.51, shared with other programs (about 10 GB in use, 6 to 21% busy); sherpa-onnx-node 2.0.1 with the DLLs of `sherpa-onnx-v2.0.1-cuda-12.x-cudnn-9.x-onnxruntime1.28.2-win-x64-cuda` and CUDA 12.9 and cuDNN 9 runtime DLLs from NVIDIA's wheels, as claude-collab set them up in `.wip/sherpa-cuda`; Nemotron Speech Streaming 0.6B 160 ms, fp32 on the GPU and int8 on the CPU; decibri 5.8.0.
- **Setup:** `.wip/asr-bench.mjs` in the voice-asr-gpu worktree loaded the built `sherpa.js` and `asr.js` with the built voice bundle's `sherpa-onnx-node` and `gpucheck.js`, in scratch homes whose `voice/models` held junctions to claude-collab's models and `sherpa-cuda`, and decoded three of claude-collab's saved 8 to 9 s clips (with 1 s of silence after each) in 160 ms chunks after one warm-up clip, timing each chunk's `accept` and `text`. Nothing was played or recorded. One home had no `sherpa-cuda`; one had the CUDA build and only `cudart64_12.dll`, `cublas64_12.dll` and `cudnn64_9.dll`, run with claude-collab's `cuda-dlls` (on this machine's PATH) taken off the PATH.
- **sherpa-onnx without CUDA:** creating a recogniser or speaker extractor with `provider: 'cuda'` when CUDA cannot load does not throw. sherpa-onnx prints `Failed to enable CUDA: … Error loading "…onnxruntime_providers_cuda.dll" which depends on "cublasLt64_12.dll" which is missing … Fallback to cpu` to stderr, with `debug` off too, and runs on the CPU. claude-collab's check of three DLLs passes that set, so it would have run the fp32 model on the CPU. The GPU check (`gpucheck.js`) read the line and the voice process chose the CPU: `CUDA did not load: cublasLt64_12.dll is missing`. With all DLLs, one speaker embedding on CUDA took 0.36 s to create and 0.44 s to compute; the whole check took 1.0 to 2.1 s.
- **Result:** GPU, two runs (and a third after loading decibri, as the voice process does at its start): real-time factor 0.40, 0.30 and 0.33; per-chunk latency median 63, 46 and 50 ms, 95th percentile 99, 76 and 86 ms, worst 134, 103 and 103 ms; the recogniser loaded in 5.5 to 6.1 s. CPU (int8, 4 threads), two runs: real-time factor 0.47 and 0.41; median 66 and 58 ms, 95th percentile 127 and 121 ms, worst 235 and 176 ms; loaded in 2.4 to 2.8 s. The GPU's transcripts matched the saved ones but for a capital, a comma and "that way" added at one clip's end; the CPU's also had "uh's" for "uhs" and "with" for "was". Loading decibri, and opening its `Speaker`, loaded no `onnxruntime.dll`; decibri loads its own only for Silero VAD or denoising (`resolveBundledOrtPath`), so the CUDA build's `onnxruntime.dll` and `onnxruntime_providers_cuda.dll` were the ones loaded.
- **Not verified:** video memory in use (claude-collab measured about 3.3 GB), dictation through the voice process with a hub, and Linux.
- **Current use:** the choice of GPU or CPU recognition (architecture.md § Dictation).

## M98. Echo check: does the voice's playback reach the mic, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** as M94; the saved captures of its echo checks (`packages/voice/test/fixtures/echo/`).
- **Method:** offline, `EchoCheck` (`packages/voice/src/echoCheck.ts`) on the five saved runs of `echo-check.mjs`, each 16 kHz capture fed in 100 ms chunks arriving 10 to 40 ms late and the played clip handed over in 20 ms pieces from 0.5 s, as the voice process does; one 2.35 s clip measured, delays from -50 ms to 1 s searched, on 10 ms RMS envelopes.
- **Speaker echo, operator silent:** every capture with no working canceller (raw, and through decibri's `tau`), on this computer (BRIO and LG speakers) and over Remote Desktop ("Remote Audio"), correlated at r 0.61 to 0.70, 100 to 270 ms after the playback timestamps, at a 90th-percentile level of -30 to -32 dBFS and 30 to 36 dB over the 20th percentile; the waveform itself is not compared, since Remote Desktop's codec and the room change it.
- **No echo:** the capture with Windows' echo cancellation on (52.6 dB below the raw run) was at -83 dBFS, with r 0.44 at a meaningless delay: too quiet to recognise, so the level, not r, decides. Synthetic steady noise at -45 dBFS spans under 6 dB.
- **Operator talking:** the captures with the operator reading aloud over the clip, echo cancelled or not, correlated at r 0.27 to 0.45 except one raw local run at 0.63, at 22 to 81 dB of range: talk hides the echo's envelope, so a measurement with talk is unclear rather than none.
- **Current use:** `EchoCheck`'s verdicts (architecture.md § Dictation).

## M99. Dictation latency against claude-collab, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.329, Haiku 5.5 (one run Opus 5.5), Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300, Node.js 24.14.0.
- **Data:** the operator's Switchboard fragments log of 2026-11-20 (12 dictated messages), a copy of the hub's store and the two sessions' transcripts, all read only; claude-collab's fragments logs of 2026-11-17 to 19, `typed` events of single-piece ends: port 8766 the operator's sessions (164), port 8795 its test wrapper's fresh sessions (28).
- **Setup:** the built `holder.js` under a scratch home, one Code Mode session in it started with `codeModeSettings` and `codeModeEnvironment` (Haiku, 120 × 40) in a scratch repository, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` in the session scratch folder with a copy of the operator's `.credentials.json`, deleted afterwards, and the built `SessionInput` driven against it through the holder client as `ClaudeConversationAdapter` wires it, reporting `steps`; four to six one-line `[spoken] [voice]` messages per run, each after the reply to the one before. Some runs resumed a copy of one of the operator's transcripts. No hub; its own share, `submitted` to the paste, is in the store's `pastedAt`.
- **Missing `sent` lines:** all 12 messages are `sent` in the store with their transcript line, and the replies were spoken. Ten have no `sent` fragment because the transcript reader confirmed them before their typing ended, and `ConversationService` skipped the fragments log for a message already `sent`. A logging gap; nothing was lost.
- **Like for like, inferred:** collab's `deliveredMs` is its Enter after the end arrived, its queue and guard waits included, and `waitedMs` its `typed`, after a 400 ms check. The operator's collab sessions pressed Enter a median 1,233 ms after the end and never under 1,065 ms, the 60 ms settle plus the 1 s limit on waiting for the paste to show in the box, which suggests the paste was not seen in time; its test wrapper's fresh sessions pressed it after 64 to 77 ms. In Switchboard, Claude took the operator's messages (the transcript line's time, which in the scratch runs below came 10 to 53 ms after the Enter) 1,179 to 1,732 ms after `submitted`, median 1,314 ms: 17 to 66 ms to the paste (the guards' two screen reads), then 1.1 to 1.7 s, consistent with the same 1 s wait; the two logged `sent` came 1,670 and 1,944 ms after `submitted`. Neither log recorded the echo or the Enter of those messages, so the shared 1 s wait, and Switchboard pressing Enter about 60 to 100 ms later than collab (the holder screen reads, about 25 to 30 ms each, where collab reads its own terminal), are inferences; queue waits, delayed timers and Claude's own delay in taking a message remain possible. The 1.7 to 1.9 s compared Switchboard's `sent` with collab's Enter.
- **Scratch session, before:** guards 45 to 66 ms, paste to echo 78 to 107 ms, Enter to transcript 418 to 453 ms (nothing read the transcript before the first screen check, 400 ms after the Enter), queued to `sent` 585 to 620 ms. A 400-line screen copy took 17 to 35 ms. **After** (transcript read every 50 ms after the Enter, the echo every 20 ms): Enter to transcript 181 to 314 ms, median 185 ms, queued to `sent` 327 to 463 ms; Claude's own line time stayed 10 to 53 ms after the Enter.
- **The 1 s wait for the echo:** in scratch sessions the paste showed within 15 to 110 ms of its write. With the parent Claude Code session's `CLAUDE_CODE_*` markers left in the environment, as a child session, it always did; with them removed, as for the hub's sessions, 4 of 15 pastes sent shortly after a reply showed after 272 to 551 ms or not within the 1 s limit (paste to Claude taking it 1,446 ms). A resumed long transcript, the operator's settings, skills and plugin folders, an async `UserPromptSubmit` hook, Opus, a 60-column or 8-row terminal and screen copies every 300 ms did not reproduce a wait every time. Whether and why the operator's sessions miss the echo is not established; the timing line says how long the echo took and, when it was not seen, whether the screen showed a menu, no input box or an empty box.
- **Current use:** the per-message timing line, the transcript and echo polling after a typed message, and the voice log's end-of-utterance line (architecture.md § Typing a message, § Dictation).

## M100. Usage and rate limits in Claude and Codex sessions, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.329 (native build; transcripts back to 2.1.220 read too), Codex CLI 0.171.0 and its `codex app-server generate-json-schema`, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Setup:** source inspection only, read-only: 473 transcript lines with `isApiErrorMessage: true` under `~/.claude/projects` (sessions of 2026-08-01 to 2026-11-20), Switchboard's hook markers, store events and `hub.log` from the 5-hour limit of 2026-11-20 (reset 1:20 PM New York time), the CLI binary's strings, the generated app-server schema, and the Codex rollouts under `~/.codex/sessions`. No session was started and no limit was forced.
- **Claude transcript:** a rejected request writes a synthetic `assistant` line (model `<synthetic>`) with `isApiErrorMessage: true`, `error: "rate_limit"`, `apiErrorStatus: 429` and the text as its content. Two kinds share `error: "rate_limit"`:
  - Usage limit: `apiError: "usage_limit_reached"`, `apiErrorParams.rate_limit_info` and `quotaLimits` with `status: "rejected"`, `resetsAt` (epoch seconds) and `rateLimitType` (`five_hour` or `seven_day`). Texts: `You've hit your 5-hour limit · resets 1:20pm (America/New_York)`, `You've hit your session limit · resets 11pm (America/New_York)` (older wording of the 5-hour limit), `You've hit your weekly limit · resets Nov 24, 4am (America/New_York)`; the time is in the named IANA zone, with minutes only when not on the hour and a date only when not today. Lines before 2.1.30x have the text but no `apiErrorParams` or `quotaLimits`.
  - Throttling: `apiErrorIsTransient: true` and `apiErrorType: "rate_limit_error"`, no reset field; text `API Error: Server is temporarily limiting requests (not your usage limit) · …`. Through an API gateway the text can end with `(reset after 14m43s)`; a gateway can also report a rate limit as `error: "server_error"`, status 503 (`auth_unavailable … rate_limit_error`).
- **Claude hook:** `StopFailure` carries `error` (`rate_limit` for both kinds), `error_details` (optional, the raw API message) and `last_assistant_message` (the text above); no reset time. `error` is also the hook's match value. Switchboard's markers of 2026-11-20 held `error: "rate_limit"` with that text as `errorMessage`.
- **Claude continues on its own:** an interactive CLI process, which a Code Mode session is, can wait for the reset itself (setting `autoContinueAtUsageLimit`, default on, behind a server-side gate; only for a rejected `five_hour` limit with a reset time and no overage). Right after the limit line it writes a `system` line, subtype `informational`: `Usage limit reached · continuing automatically at 1:20pm · esc to cancel`. After the reset it writes `Usage limit reset · Continuing automatically` and a meta `user` line with `origin: {kind: "auto-continuation"}`: "Your claude.ai usage limit has reset. Continue the task you were working on when the limit was reached; do not repeat work that is already complete." Esc cancels the wait, and `/rate-limit-options` arms it again. A weekly limit and a throttle are not waited on.
- **Code Mode sessions on 2026-11-20:** eleven held sessions logged the waiting line between 12:34 and 12:38 PM, by two routes. A turn already running got a meta note (`Usage limit reached; a short grace allowance remains, then this turn is cut off without warning. …`), ended with `Stop` (`idle`, no `rate_limit` anywhere in the markers) and then logged the waiting line; a turn whose first request was refused ended with `StopFailure` `rate_limit`. The five still held in their first process at the reset (work items 626, 628, 629, 630 and 633) continued by themselves 71 to 93 s after it, with `running` markers at 1:21:11 to 1:21:32 PM. Five setup sessions started after the limit (work items 632, 634, 635, 636, 637) failed their first request 1 to 2 s after the start, and the end rule released each 1 to 2 s later, so the wait died with the process and each work item failed with "Setup ended without a worktree." The operator reopened two of them (632 at 12:53 PM, before the reset; 635 at 1:56 PM): Open in terminal resumed each in a new process with no prompt, which has no wait, and each went on only when the operator typed "continue" (1:55 and 1:56 PM). A task session that failed meanwhile left its work item `blocked` with "The turn failed: rate_limit." until it continued.
- **Codex app-server (schema only):** `turn/completed` with `turn.status: "failed"` and `turn.error.codexErrorInfo` `usageLimitExceeded` or `rateLimitExceeded` (`serverOverloaded` separately), the readable text in `turn.error.message`; an `error` notification with `willRetry` comes first. `account/rateLimits/updated` sends a sparse `RateLimitSnapshot` with `primary` and `secondary` windows (`usedPercent`, `windowDurationMins`, `resetsAt` in epoch seconds) and `rateLimitReachedType`; `account/rateLimits/read` answers the full snapshot and `ordinaryUsageAllowed`, which the schema says to rely on instead of inferring recovery from percentages or reset times. The CLI's messages read `You've hit your usage limit. … or try again at <time>.` or `… Try again later.`. No rollout under `~/.codex/sessions` held a usage-limit failure.
- **Codex exec (legacy):** `turn.failed` carries only `error.message`.
- **Live check, 2026-11-20:** two interactive `claude --model haiku` processes (not Code Mode, which a non-default `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` turns off) in node-pty, with a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` holding copies of the operator's `.credentials.json` and `.claude.json` (removed afterwards) and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` at a local proxy that passed requests to `api.anthropic.com` and, while switched on, answered `/v1/messages` with 429, `rate_limit_error` and `anthropic-ratelimit-unified-status: rejected`, `-representative-claim: five_hour`, `-reset: <epoch s>` and `-overage-status: rejected`. After a normal turn each refused prompt gave `StopFailure` with `error: "rate_limit"`, `last_assistant_message: "You've hit your 5-hour limit · resets 2:19pm (America/New_York)"` (the reset was 2:19:42, so the text cuts it to the minute) and no `error_details`; the transcript line had `apiErrorParams.rate_limit_info.resetsAt` to the second and the waiting line; the screen showed `⎿ You've hit your 5-hour limit · resets 2:19pm (America/New_York)`, `● Usage limit reached · continuing automatically at 2:19pm · esc to cancel` and under the input box `⚠ Usage limit reached · limit resets 2:19pm` / `Continuing automatically at 2:19pm · Esc to cancel`. A message pasted and sent during the wait was refused again and re-armed it (`Usage limit reached again · continuing automatically at 2:19pm`); it did not cancel it. After the reset the proxy passed requests again, and each process continued by itself, 47 and 91 s after the reset, with a `UserPromptSubmit` whose prompt was the continue text.
- **Not verified:** a Code Mode or background session's own screen at the limit; whether a `claude --bg` background session waits, since the release stops it at the failed turn; the CLI's wait when its server-side gate is off; a Codex limit live, and whether a failed thread accepts `turn/start` right after the reset.
- **Current use:** reading limits and continuing sessions after the reset (architecture.md § Rate limits, PRD R111).

## M101. Mouse reporting after a replay, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** @xterm/headless 6.0.0, @xterm/addon-serialize 0.14.0, node-pty 1.1.0, Codex CLI 0.171.0, Claude Code CLI 2.1.330, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Symptom:** moving the mouse over a VS Code terminal attached to a held Codex session typed `[MC` followed by raw bytes, again and again, into Codex's input box, after Codex showed "Conversation interrupted". These are legacy mouse motion reports (`ESC [ M Cb Cx Cy`, `Cb` = `C`, motion with no button) that Codex read as Esc and typed text.
- **Experiment:** a headless terminal fed `?1000h ?1002h ?1003h ?1015h ?1006h`, the sequence crossterm's mouse capture writes, was serialized and the result written into a fresh headless terminal; then the same through the built holder, with a real ConPTY running a Node.js child in raw mode that wrote that sequence, and an `Attachment` client whose received replay was written into a headless terminal. The Codex binary's strings hold `?1006h` and `?1006l`; Claude Code's hold `?1000h ?1006h`, its own mouse mode.
- **Result:**
  - `SerializeAddon` writes the tracking mode (`?9h`, `?1000h`, `?1002h` or `?1003h`, from `modes.mouseTrackingMode`) but not the encoding. The fresh terminal ended with tracking `any` and encoding `DEFAULT`, so it sends legacy reports; the program had `SGR`. xterm.js ignores 1005 and 1015, and keeps the encoding (`DEFAULT`, `SGR`, `SGR_PIXELS`) only in its internal mouse service (`_core.coreMouseService.activeEncoding`); its public API does not expose it.
  - ConPTY passed `?1003h ?1006h` through only from a child in raw mode (virtual terminal input on); from a child in cooked mode it passed only `?1015h`. ConPTY also writes `?9001h` and `?1004h` at start.
  - Through the holder the attached client ended with `any` and `DEFAULT` before the fix, and `any` and `SGR` with the encoding added after the replay.
  - A terminal that had `any` tracking, `SGR_PIXELS` and focus reports on before the replay kept them after a replay of a program with none: the serialized screen only turns modes on, and the attach client reuses its terminal when it attaches again after an exit or a lost connection.
- **Current use:** the holder's replay (architecture.md § Protocol, Attach). `snapshot` is unchanged, because its readers are headless terminals that send no input.
- **Not verified:** a live Codex session attached in VS Code's terminal after the fix; Linux.

## M102. Answering a permission prompt and an AskUserQuestion form with keys, 2026-11-21

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.330 (Haiku), classic renderer (`tui: "default"`), @xterm/headless 6.0.0, node-pty 1.1.0, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Experiment:** an interactive `claude --permission-mode default --model haiku --settings {"tui":"default", …}` in a node-pty of 110 by 40 in a scratch folder, with a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` holding a copy of the sign-in (deleted after), its screen read through a headless terminal. Code Mode was off for the run (its start dialog answered No); the prompts were the plain tool calls'. Three prompts: run `echo spike > spike-out.txt` with Bash; one AskUserQuestion call with two single-choice questions; one with a multi-select question. Each key was written alone, the screen read 2.5 s later.
- **Result:**
  - The Bash permission prompt draws below a rule: "Bash command", the description, the command between dashed rules, "Do you want to proceed?", the options `❯ 1. Yes`, `2. Yes, and always allow access to <folder> from this project` (wrapped), `3. Yes, and switch to auto mode · …`, `4. No`, then "Esc to cancel · Tab to amend". Writing `1` ran the command at once, with no Enter: the file was written and the turn ended.
  - The AskUserQuestion form draws a tab strip `←  ☐ Color  ☐ Size  ✔ Submit  →`, the question, the options numbered with their descriptions under them, `3. Type something.`, a rule, `4. Chat about this`, and "Enter to select · Tab/Arrow keys to navigate · Esc to cancel". A digit picked a single-choice option and moved to the next question (the strip's box turned to ☒); after the last, a review step listed the answers with `❯ 1. Submit answers` and `2. Cancel`, and `1` submitted: "Answered Claude's questions: · Pick a color → Blue · Pick a size → Small".
  - In a multi-select question a digit toggled that option's `[ ]`/`[✔]` and stayed on the question; Enter toggled the option under the cursor rather than submitting; the strip's → reaches Submit.
- **Current use:** the web page's answers to such prompts by digits, Escape and → (architecture.md § Web page); the screens recorded here, scrubbed, are the fixtures in `packages/conversation/test/fixtures/prompts`.
- **Not verified:** a prompt raised from a Code Mode `REPL` script, including the auto mode countdown prompt (M80); Tab to amend; Linux; narrower terminals that wrap the options further.

## M103. A Chrome window glued over an installed web app's column, 2026-11-21

- **Historical:** the native Chrome pane and its pane helper were removed on 2026-11-24, with the dock; the web page's embedded link tabs (PRD R125) replace them.
- **Versions:** Chrome 157.0.8103.52 (window tests) and Chrome for Testing 155.0.8059.12 (extension tests), .NET 10, Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300, three monitors at 125%, 100% and 150%.
- **Experiment:** a throwaway PerMonitorV2 helper moved a scratch Chrome instance's windows (scratch `--user-data-dir`): an `--app=` window standing in for the installed page, which has the same class, frame and title bar, and a second, normal tabbed window as the pane, found by marker titles. A test MV3 extension opened the pane window and switched its tabs on messages over a WebSocket from the helper. Spike report: `.wip/artifacts/chat/research-chrome-pane.md` in claude-collab; code in the `spike-chrome-pane` worktree's `.wip/chrome-pane/`.
- **Result:**
  - A visible `Chrome_WidgetWin_1` window's title is `<page title> - Google Chrome` for a normal window, following the active tab. An app window's title was the page title alone for the spike's `--app` window in Chrome 157, `Switchboard - <page title>` for the installed page in the operator's Chrome, and the app's name alone, not following `document.title`, in Chrome for Testing 155 (below). Every window of a profile is in one browser process.
  - `SetWindowLongPtr(pane, GWLP_HWNDPARENT, app)` made the pane owned; it stayed below the app until raised once with `SetWindowPos(HWND_TOP, SWP_NOACTIVATE)`, then stayed directly above it. Minimizing the app hid the pane and restoring showed it; closing the app destroyed the pane and its tabs.
  - One `SetWindowPos` that moved and resized together left the child `Intermediate D3D Window` offset inside the pane in 25 of 28 trials, drawing the page shifted or blank; a resize with `SWP_NOMOVE` and a move with `SWP_NOSIZE` left it at 0,0 in 24 of 24. Moves between monitors of different scale still offset it (6 of 8), because Chrome resizes its window on `WM_DPICHANGED`; aligning again 300 ms after the last change and moving that child back to 0,0 with `SetWindowPos` fixed all 8.
  - A normal window would not go below about 514 DIP wide (643 px at 125%), so the column must be at least about 515 CSS px.
  - The page's viewport origin is the DWM frame's left plus half the side border and its bottom minus that border and `innerHeight × devicePixelRatio`; `Chrome_RenderWidgetHostHWND` exists only after the window had the focus.
  - Activating the pane made it the foreground window and the app page's `document.hasFocus()` false.
  - An extension service worker with a WebSocket that carried a message every 20 s stayed alive 188 s. `chrome.windows.create({focused: false})` still took the foreground.
  - 2026-11-21, Chrome for Testing 155.0.8059.12 and the operator's Chrome: an app window's title is the app's name, not the page's title, and does not follow `document.title`: `127.0.0.1_/page/` for an `--app=http://127.0.0.1:<port>/page/` window, with or without a manifest, and `Switchboard` for the installed page, whose AppUserModelID is `Chrome._crx_<shortened app ID>.<profile>`. So a marker in the page's title never reached its window's title, and the dock's search failed on the operator's machine.
  - 2026-11-21, Chrome for Testing 155: `DwmSetWindowAttribute(DWMWA_CLOAK)` on a window of another process fails with `0x80070005` (access denied); `ShowWindow(SW_HIDE)` and `ShowWindow(SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE)` hid and showed the owned pane window in place, and a move of the app window by 175, 75 px moved the pane by the same.
  - 2026-11-21, Chrome for Testing 155: unlike the cloak, `DwmSetWindowAttribute(DWMWA_TRANSITIONS_FORCEDISABLED)` on a window of another process succeeds (`S_OK`), which turns off the fade `ShowWindow(SW_HIDE)` otherwise plays; `SetWindowPos` with `SWP_HIDEWINDOW` and `SWP_SHOWWINDOW` also hid and showed it without taking the foreground, and an empty `SetWindowRgn` was taken too. Whether the fade is gone was not checked on screen.
  - 2026-11-21, Chrome for Testing 155 at 125%: `WM_NCHITTEST` on an app window answers `HTCLIENT` up to the last pixel of its visible frame and `HTRIGHT` or `HTBOTTOM` only in the invisible border outside it (8 px), so a pane whose window rectangle, invisible borders included, reached past the page window's frame covered the page window's resize borders; inset by the pane's own invisible border, the pane's window rectangle ended exactly at the page window's frame.
  - 2026-11-21, the operator's Chrome: after a pane attempt failed, the extension loaded the next marker into its existing pane window's marker tab as `pane.html#<new marker>`, a fragment-only change, which is a same-document navigation that runs no script, so the window kept its earlier marker's title (`Switchboard pane <old marker>`) and the helper never found it. In Chrome for Testing 155, loading `pane.html?marker=<marker>` with another query in the same tab titled the window with the new marker each time. The operator's Chrome showed the installed page's title in its window title (`Switchboard - (2) Switchboard [sb-pane <marker>]`), unlike Chrome for Testing 155's app windows.
  - 2026-11-21 15:55, the operator's Chrome: the pane window was titled `Switchboard pane - Google Chrome`, with no marker, and the installed page's window `Switchboard`, after the Chrome extension's `dist` was rebuilt: an unpacked extension's pages load the rebuilt files while its service worker keeps the code it started with until the extension is reloaded, so the old worker loaded `pane.html#<marker>` into a page whose new script read only `?marker=`.
- **Superseded:** R125 replaces the native pane and its helper with extension-hosted link tabs; these measurements remain historical evidence.
- **Not verified:** the operator's branded Chrome with the extension and a real installed app together; the pane's own window buttons, Snap layouts, virtual desktops, sleep and Chrome restarts; whether session restore brings back a pane closed with its owner. The trial counts are small and from one Chrome version.

## M104. Repeated lines after resizing a held session, 2026-11-21

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.330 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; Node.js 24.14.0, node-pty 1.1.0 (ConPTY), @xterm/headless 6.0.0.
- **Symptom:** the web page's console showed Claude Code's banner five times stacked, and earlier conversation lines a second time, after the session had been resized several times.
- **Experiment:** `.wip/resize-exp/exp.cjs` (not committed) spawned `claude --model haiku --settings {"tui":"default"}` with `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE=1` in node-pty at 120×40, on a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` holding a copy of the operator's sign-in (deleted after), and wrote its output into a headless terminal with 10,000 lines of scrollback, resizing that terminal after the output already queued as the holder does. It asked for the numbers 1 to 80, one per line, so the conversation outgrew the screen, then resized the pseudo-terminal: once to 100 columns, once to the same size, four times between 110 and 100 columns 250 ms apart, once to 30 rows, and four times between 90 and 100 columns 40 ms apart. After each step it counted the banners and the number lines that appeared more than once in the headless buffer. A second run set xterm.js's `windowsPty: {backend: 'conpty', buildNumber: 26300}`.
- **Result:**
  - Before any resize every number showed once. Each change of columns repeated about one line at the screen's top edge in the scrollback: 3 repeated lines after the first resize, 7 after the next four, 11 after the last four. A resize to the same size, which the holder skips, and a change of rows alone repeated none.
  - The output held one banner throughout: Claude did not reprint its history on a resize. With a conversation shorter than the screen, every resize redrew a clean screen.
  - `windowsPty` gave the same 11 repeated lines.
  - The repeated lines are in the holder's own buffer, which the console's replay serializes and every attached terminal shows; the console writes its stream after a reset, once.
- **Not verified:** Linux; the cause inside ConPTY or Claude Code's renderer; a banner repeated by a resize while it was at the screen's top edge, which the symptom suggests.
- **Current use:** when the web page's console gives the session its size (architecture.md § Web page).

## M105. Context size and auto-compact threshold in Claude Code and Codex, 2026-11-21

Source inspection of the Claude Code VS Code extension 2.1.328 (`extension.js`, `webview/index.js`), the operator's Claude Code transcripts, and `codex app-server generate-json-schema --experimental` of Codex CLI 0.171.0.

- Claude Code's panel counts a request's size as `input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens + cache_read_input_tokens + output_tokens` of its latest assistant message, and its context indicator puts the auto-compact threshold at `contextWindow - min(maxOutputTokens, 20000) - 13000`, with `contextWindow` and `maxOutputTokens` from the result's `modelUsage`. The indicator shows only once less than half remains, as a 20 px pie, with "N% of context remaining until auto-compact." and "Click to compact now." on hover; a click compacts.
- Claude Code's model table gives each model's `context.window` and `max_output_tokens.default`: 200,000 for the Claude 3.x, Haiku 4.5, Sonnet 4.x and Opus 4.0 to 4.6 models, 1,000,000 natively for Opus 4.7 and later, Sonnet 5 and later, Haiku 5.5, Fable and Mythos, with 32,000 to 128,000 output. Sonnet 3.7 and 4.x and Opus 4.6 reach 1,000,000 with the `[1m]` suffix or the 1M beta.
- Transcripts record the API model ID without the `[1m]` suffix: an `opus` session at 515,000 tokens wrote `claude-opus-5-5`. `compact_boundary` lines carry `compactMetadata.trigger`, `preTokens` and, in newer versions, `postTokens`. Two automatic compactions of `claude-opus-5-5` sessions happened at 966,902 and 969,719 tokens, at the formula's 967,000; others happened between 207,000 and 470,000, which settings such as `autoCompactWindow` (per model, or `auto` for "the window tuned for the model") and `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` move, so the formula is an upper bound.
- Codex's app-server sends `thread/tokenUsage/updated {threadId, turnId, tokenUsage: {total, last, modelContextWindow}}`, each breakdown `{inputTokens, cachedInputTokens, outputTokens, reasoningOutputTokens, totalTokens}`; rollouts record `model_context_window` (258,400 for the operator's model). It reports no auto-compact threshold.

Current use: [architecture.md](architecture.md) § Context meter.

## M106. Claude Code's terminal colours, 2026-11-21

Source inspection of Claude Code 2.1.330 (`claude.exe`), and its output in a pseudo-terminal at 100 by 30 with `COLORTERM=truecolor` and the default theme, recorded with a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.

- Claude Code draws for its own theme setting (`theme`: `dark` by default, `light`, their colour-blind and `-ansi` variants), never for the terminal's colours. The `dark` theme's colours are 24-bit: a sent message's row `48;2;55;55;55` with its text `38;2;255;255;255`, the logo's background `48;2;0;0;0`, secondary text `38;2;153;153;153` and the mode line `38;2;255;193;7`. Only the `-ansi` themes use the palette.
- A terminal's palette therefore cannot recolour the `dark` theme: on a light background its message rows and logo stay dark blocks with white text, and its grey and amber text falls to about 2.8:1 and 1.6:1 against #F8F8F8.

Current use: superseded by M126 on 2026-11-22: the web page's console no longer keeps Dark Modern's colours in both schemes, and rewrites Claude Code's dark theme colours in the light scheme.

## M107. xterm.js's scrollbar and textarea after a font change, 2026-11-21

Source inspection of @xterm/xterm 6.0.0 (`src/browser/Viewport.ts`, `CoreBrowserTerminal.ts`, `services/RenderService.ts`) and the web page's console in headless Chromium 1247 at 125 % display scale, its pane 680 by 1190 CSS px.

- Changing `fontSize` resizes the renderer and redraws the rows at once, but the viewport sizes its scrollbar (`setScrollDimensions` in `_sync`) only when the buffer resizes, a buffer activates or the buffer scrolls, and the helper textarea follows the cursor only when the cursor moves (`_syncTextArea` on `onCursorMove`). A font change alone, as when the console's pane shrinks and it refits, leaves both at the old font's size: a 90-row screen refitted from 1190 to 600 px drew in 576 px, while its scrollbar track stayed 1080 px tall with the thumb placed for that height, and the textarea sat 1080 px down. The console's pane, which then scrolled, showed the track running past the screen with its thumb in the middle and content above and below it.
- The same-size `resize` returns early and fires nothing, so no public call syncs them; the console calls the private `_core._viewport.queueSync()` and, after the next frame, `_core._syncTextArea()`.
- Output that scrolls the buffer or moves the cursor syncs them, so the fault showed only on a session idle after a refit.

Current use: the console's resync after each fit ([architecture.md](architecture.md) § Web page); `packages/web/check-console.mjs` checks the console's layout after its pane shrinks.

## M108. Codex's terminal app on a thread the hub drives, 2026-11-21

- **Versions:** Codex CLI 0.171.0 (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\OpenAI\Codex\bin\codex.exe`), node-pty 1.1.0 and @xterm/headless 6.0.0, Node.js 24.14.0, Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Setup:** scratch Codex homes with a copy of the sign-in file, deleted after each run, and a trusted scratch repository; `codex app-server --listen unix://` in the foreground on the home's control socket, because the agent's shell is in a job object that refuses `app-server daemon start` ("this Windows launcher prevents background processes from outliving it"); `--listen unix://<path>` elsewhere was refused, "socket directory is not private to the current user". An API client as the hub's (`app-server proxy` with WebSocket framing, `initialize` with `experimentalApi`) started the thread; the terminal app ran in a node-pty of 120 by 36 with `TERM=xterm-256color`, its output written into a headless terminal. The operator's Codex home, daemon, hub, holder and sessions were not touched.
- **Two clients on one thread: pass.** `codex resume --remote unix://<home>/app-server-control/app-server-control.sock <thread>` showed the thread's history within 10 s and every turn live, from either side. A message typed in it reached the API as a `userMessage` with the terminal's own `clientId`. An approval request reached both clients; either could answer, and the other got `serverRequest/resolved` and closed its prompt. Plain `codex resume <thread>` also joined the running server; `--no-daemon` was locked out: "This conversation is open in another app".
- **No turn yet: fails.** On a thread started with no turn the terminal exited with 1 within 2 s: "thread/resume failed: no rollout found for thread id …". `thread/turns/list` on such a thread answers "thread … is not materialized yet; thread/turns/list is unavailable before first user message". After one turn it joins.
  - Current use: the hub reads either refusal as an empty conversation, not an unavailable one (`notMaterialized` in `packages/codex`), and its next poll tries again until the thread has a turn ([architecture.md](architecture.md) § Web page).
- **`--no-alt-screen`:** no alternate screen and no mouse reporting (only `?1004h`, `?2004h`, `?2026`, `?25`, and ConPTY's `?9001h`); finished output scrolls into the scrollback and a resize redraws cleanly. Full screen turns on `?1049h`, `?1003h` and `?1006h` (M101), and a resize left cells of the old frame beside the new.
- **Keys:** Esc mid-turn interrupts the turn; Ctrl+C mid-turn offers Cancel task, Run in background and Exit; Ctrl+C at an idle prompt exits at once with code 0, "Disconnected from this task. Any running work continues.". A terminal killed with an approval pending, or with the server killed under it ("Reconnecting to server…"), left the thread loaded and its request answerable from the API.
- **Settings the terminal changes: sticky for the thread.** Each change came as `thread/settings/updated {threadId, threadSettings}`, whose settings hold `model`, `effort`, `approvalPolicy`, `approvalsReviewer`, `sandboxPolicy` and `collaborationMode {mode, settings}`; the hub's next `turn/start`, which names none of them, ran under the changed ones.
  - The first turn typed in the terminal set `collaborationMode.settings.developer_instructions` to Codex's "Collaboration Mode: Default" text and `serviceTier` to `default`, leaving the approval policy and sandbox as the hub set them. The thread's own `developerInstructions`, a code word, still held for the terminal's turn and the hub's next one.
  - `/permissions` took effect when chosen, before any turn: a read-only thread became `workspaceWrite` with `on-request`. On a home with no `[windows] sandbox` setting it first asked to set up Codex's Windows sandbox, and the non-admin choice wrote `[windows] sandbox = "unelevated"` to the home's `config.toml`.
  - `/model` with Enter ("enter default · s session") changed the thread's model and effort and also wrote `model` and `model_reasoning_effort` to the home's `config.toml`, the default of every later Codex start that sets no model.
  - Shift+Tab switched `collaborationMode.mode` to `plan`, with Codex's Plan Mode instructions; the hub's next turn ran in Plan mode under the terminal's model.
- **Memory:** one `codex.exe` per terminal, no children, 109 to 110 MB working set and 23 MB private (110 by 30, idle and after a turn); a held Code Mode session is 430 to 480 MB (M82).
- **Unload:** a thread stayed loaded while the API client was subscribed, with or without a terminal. The server's log names the rule, a thread that "has no subscribers and is idle; shutting down"; when a session's thread unloads once both clients leave was not measured.
- **Side effects:** the terminal wrote `[tui] screen_reader_detection_done = true` into the home's `config.toml`.
- **Not verified:** the managed daemon itself; a terminal reconnecting to a restarted daemon; several terminals over hours; a `request_user_input` question from the terminal's side; an eye check in the web console; Linux.
- **Current use:** the web page's console on a Codex app-server session and the notes on settings changed outside Switchboard ([architecture.md](architecture.md) § Codex runner, Codex terminal in the holder). Re-run after upgrading Codex.

## M109. The diff viewer under the web page's CSP, 2026-11-21

- **Setup:** `packages/web`'s built `dist/diff/` (@pierre/diffs 1.6.4, Shiki 4.6.0) served under `/page/diff/<build>/` with the web page's CSP (`PAGE_CSP`), with the page's `theme.css` and `page.css`, by a stand-in page whose own module script imports `diff.js` and calls `mountDiff`; headless Chromium through `playwright-cli`, the system scheme light. Cases: `packages/web/src/main.ts` from 3,108 to 3,438 lines with git's 588-line patch, and a one-line change of the 14,127-line `package-lock.json`.
- **Result:** no CSP or script error: `import()` of the viewer, its module workers (`new Worker(new URL('./worker.js', import.meta.url), {type: 'module'})`) and their grammar and theme chunks all load as the page's own scripts. `main.ts` showed split at 1,280 px with 69 rows in the DOM and 712 coloured tokens once the workers answered, folded unchanged regions with their handles, and word highlights; at 375 px it showed stacked and wrapped in 12 px type. The lock file showed its changed line uncoloured, past the 10,000-line cap. No long task was observed in either case.
- **Not verified:** dark mode, a live hub and page (the diff route, the tabs and the installed folder together), and a real phone.
- **Current use:** the web page's Changes tab ([architecture.md](architecture.md) § Web page, Diffs). Re-run after upgrading @pierre/diffs or Shiki.

## M110. VS Code's file icon lookup, 2026-11-21

- **Versions:** VS Code 1.147.0 (commit `8a14f0cc1b08d531eb83255ea3764ae22aa932b5`), its built-in `theme-seti` extension (`vs-seti-icon-theme.json`, `seti.woff`).
- **Source inspection:** the install's `out/vs/workbench/workbench.desktop.main.js`. A file's label gets the classes `<name>-name-file-icon`, `name-file-icon`, `<ending>-ext-file-icon` for each dot-separated ending of the lower-case name, longest first (`a.test.ts` gives `test.ts` and `ts`; `.gitignore` gives `gitignore`), `ext-file-icon`, and `<language>-lang-file-icon`. The theme's rules are CSS selectors built from them: a `fileNames` key selects its name class, `name-file-icon` and its name's ending classes; a `fileExtensions` key one class per segment and `ext-file-icon`; a `languageIds` key its language class alone. So a file name beats any ending, a longer ending beats a shorter one, and an ending beats the language; a theme with `json` and no `jsonc` gives `jsonc` the json icon. The `light` section's rules add `.vs`, so a key it names takes its light definition in a light theme and the others keep theirs. The language of a file by its path (`guessLanguageIdByFilepathOrFirstLine`) lower-cases the path, then takes an exact file name, else the longest `filenamePatterns` glob, matched case-insensitively against the whole path when it holds a separator and the name otherwise, else the longest `extensions` ending, scanning registrations from the last, so the later one wins a tie; only then the first line.
- **Not verified:** the page's icons beside VS Code's Explorer by eye; high contrast themes, whose rules this theme does not give; user `files.associations`, which the page cannot see.
- **Current use:** the web page's file type icons ([architecture.md](architecture.md) § Web page, File type icons). Re-run after upgrading VS Code, then `gen-seti.mjs`.

## M111. Voice on a phone through the web page, 2026-11-21

- **Versions:** Windows 11, Node.js 24.14.0, HeadlessChrome 155 (Playwright), the sherpa-onnx CPU int8 recogniser; Switchboard at `473f2df9`. iOS 27's behaviour is from WebKit's release notes, bugs and developer reports, not a device.
- **Setup:** a throwaway prototype (`.wip/phone-voice-spike/` on the `phone-voice-spike` branch): a stand-in for the page origin with `pageOrigin`-style checks, pairing tokens, `sb_page` cookies, a per-device voice flag only this computer could set and a binary WebSocket; a phone-backed `Capture` and `Output` plugged into the unchanged `VoiceService` and `Dictation`; a page that opens the mic with echo cancellation, noise suppression and AGC and sets `navigator.audioSession.type = 'play-and-record'` in one tap, resamples to 16 kHz Int16 in 20 ms frames in an AudioWorklet, and plays 24 kHz PCM through a worklet with a start buffer and an immediate flush. Headless Chromium read a saved clip of the operator as its fake mic (`--use-file-for-fake-audio-capture`, `--mute-audio`); speech was `packages/voice/test/fixtures/echo/played.wav` streamed at three times real time. Nothing was played aloud and no microphone of the computer was opened; a scratch home only.
- **Result:** recognition gave "I have a really weird issue where mouse movement over the VS code window" for the clip, the same with a simulated cellular downlink (40 ms plus 0 to 120 ms jitter). Uplink: 1,098 frames, no gaps, 263 kbit/s, page to server p50 0.6 ms, p95 20 ms, max 83 ms (bursty worklet delivery). End of speech to `utterance` 3.57 s, the 3 s pause with the turn model off. First reply audio sent to Web Audio's estimated output 45 to 50 ms on loopback (60 ms start buffer, 42 ms output latency), 96 to 167 ms with one underrun per reply on the simulated downlink, 151 to 280 ms with no underruns with a 200 ms start buffer; stop to estimated silence 47 to 50 ms, 111 to 200 ms simulated. An interrupted reply was reported at 906 ms while the page had played 784 to 820 ms, so a position counted from bytes written overstates what was heard on a remote speaker. The refusal matrix passed against the stand-in (17 of 17): no cookie 401; a foreign or missing `Origin`, a rebinding host, a proxy header with a loopback `Host`, `http`, another tailnet login, a Funnel request and `X-Forwarded-Proto: http` 403; a local token through the remote host 403 and used up; a paired phone before voice was allowed 403; the phone calling the allow route 403; a second good socket closed the first with 4001.
- **iOS, from sources:** a home-screen app asks for the microphone at every launch (WebKit bug 327237), and `AudioContext.resume()` must run in the tap; a hidden page mutes capture, suspends the context and soon loses its socket; echo cancellation covers the page's own Web Audio output, but the loudspeaker drops 12 to 30 dB with the mic open, playback is downsampled to mono (bug 311451), iOS 27 has an open crackle regression with echo cancellation on, and turning it off routes to the earpiece (bug 326286); AirPods drop to the hands-free profile with the mic open; `play-and-record` before `getUserMedia` keeps output on the loudspeaker; the hardware runs at 48 kHz and a 16 kHz context loses samples (bug 251350), so the page resamples itself; AudioWorklet since iOS 14.5; the installed app has its own cookie jar.
- **Not verified:** anything on an iPhone: WebSocket upgrades and the `Tailscale-User-*` headers through `tailscale serve`, Safari matching `wss:` against the page's `connect-src 'self'`, the loudspeaker's route and loudness, the crackle, AirPods, real input and output latency, the voice check's scores on phone audio, and reopening the mic within a launch. Output times are `getOutputTimestamp` estimates with audio muted; only the downlink was delayed; the prototype ran full duplex with no voiceprint or watchers and its stand-in had no page key.
- **Current use:** voice on a phone ([architecture.md](architecture.md) § Remote access, Voice on a phone). Re-check on the phone at each slice's live check and after an iOS upgrade.

## M112. Linux shared features and Codex acceptance, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Linux x86_64 on Wayland, Node.js 24.22.1, .NET SDK 10.0.115, Codex CLI and isolated managed daemon 0.171.0; VS Code integration host 1.147.0. No live Claude session was run.
- **Setup:** isolated hub apps, settings, scratch folders and Codex homes; temporary sign-in copies removed afterwards. No source polling or writes. Drivers and logs are under `.wip/linux-verification/` in the Linux verification checkouts.
- **Passed:** live Codex replies, paginated history, resume and reconnect; interrupting one thread leaves another working, an interrupted thread accepts a follow-up, and a turn survives client disconnect. Shared hub routes keep `Repo` and `repo` separate, consolidate symlink retries, refuse a send from another folder, stream the reply and an owner/file artifact, and retain the reply across a hub app restart. Seven Linux file-link checks passed. The real VS Code integration suite passed 108 tests. After platform-specific test corrections and excluding CUDA/TensorRT providers from the CPU voice bundle, `npm run verify` passed, including 636 dock tests. The packaged CPU ONNX runtime loaded Silero VAD.
- **Audio and desktop:** default microphone capture delivered data. With the distribution's `pactl` installed in `~/.local/bin`, the real other-recorder watcher detected capture starting and ending. The installed GNOME service answered `Terminals` but lacked `TerminalUnfocused` (M114 later saw the newer extension emit `TerminalUnfocused` in an isolated GNOME Shell; the installed service was not reloaded, so this result stands for it). The default voice models directory was absent (superseded by M113, which installed the models).
- **Limits:** a model-driven artifact probe timed out once; a retry passed. Closing a hub app exposed a deferred-read race against its closed database. A shutdown guard passed both resolved/rejected-read regression cases, and the live artifact/restart probe then completed without those errors. No audible playback, speech recognition, voiceprint enrolment, room hold, spoken commands, barge-in, duplex or GPU recognition acceptance; no lock/sleep transition or graphical terminal, browser board or dock presentation acceptance. Passing injected tests does not prove these live paths.
- **Current use:** Linux verification of Codex, shared conversations, voice capture and microphone-use monitoring. Full desktop voice acceptance still needs the remaining live checks.

## M113. Installing Linux voice models, 2026-11-20

- **Sources:** sherpa-onnx's `asr-models` release, the 160 ms int8 Nemotron export dated 2026-04-25; its `speaker-recongition-models` release, WeSpeaker ResNet34 LM; LiveKit's multilingual turn detector `v0.4.1-intl`, pinned to `87e35fcb1e60a569bea70346191c4886ea92e281`. LiveKit's current `main` lacks the `onnx/model_q8.onnx` layout this runtime reads. Download addresses and SHA-256 checks are in [install-linux.md](install-linux.md).
- **Setup:** the M112 Linux machine and Node.js 24.22.1, packaged sherpa-onnx-node 2.0.1 and ONNX Runtime 1.31.1; about 857 MiB downloaded, 1.1 GB installed under `~/.switchboard/voice/models`. Release SHA-256 digests and LiveKit LFS digests matched before installation. Existing voiceprints and builds were preserved.
- **Passed:** Switchboard's CPU recogniser transcribed the recorded `played.wav` fixture as "Tests passed and the new build is ready"; the speaker adapter computed a finite 256-value embedding from the same clip; the turn worker loaded its pinned tokenizer and ONNX model and returned probability `0.4373558759689331` for "That is everything. Thank you.", using English threshold `0.011`. The turn worker remained a separate process from sherpa-onnx. These are real native inference checks, not fake runners. Drivers, release metadata and results are under `.wip/linux-setup/` in the Linux fixes worktree.
- **Limits:** no live spoken phrase, speaker enrolment, barge-in, room-hold or acoustic quality test. The TTS credential was absent; speech playback was not tested. No operator voice process or desktop was restarted.
- **Current use:** the Linux CPU voice models and their installation ([install-linux.md](install-linux.md)).

## M114. Linux terminal and desktop acceptance, 2026-11-20

- **Versions:** Claude Code 2.1.329, GNOME Shell/Mutter 50.1, Node.js 24.22.1, VS Code integration build 1.147.0. Isolated homes, config folders, Claude runtime sockets and credentials; no source writes or operator-daemon changes.
- **Code Mode:** the Linux pseudo-terminal writes absolute-column moves (`CSI G`) between banner words. Removing them joined the words and made every start fail even with the positive banner on screen. Reading them as spacing, as with forward moves (`CSI C`), passes the captured-banner and encoded-refusal regressions. Direct subscription model turns were rate-limited; these parser checks do not prove acting turns.
- **Held-session mechanics:** the real built hub and holder passed attach replay, typing/paste/control keys, stage/worktree reports from Bash mode, `/clear` identity and old-ID refusal, idle termination/resume, hub restart with a command and subsequent report, holder death/recovery, Stop/Enter resume, attached-terminal end deferral, deletion, orphan listing/stopping and trust refusals. The ordinary background start reached the model limit and became blocked with `rate_limit`. Detailed evidence is `.wip/linux-acceptance/claude/report.md`.
- **Desktop:** a separate headless GNOME compositor loaded the current extension. A real Ptyxis window accepted virtual-keyboard text. Focusing and leaving it emitted `TerminalFocused` and `TerminalUnfocused`. The real dock, showing the recorded view over a loopback fixture hub, occupied `(0,32,320,1048)`, moved to the right edge on a title drag, and widened from 320 to 421 pixels while keeping that edge. This proves native placement/input and fixture presentation, not real ticket actions. The operator's desktop was not reloaded.
- **Audio:** real default-device playback/drain and concurrent echo-cancelled capture passed with the recorded 24 kHz speech clip (112800 playback bytes, 70400 capture bytes). Captured audio was discarded. This is device transport, not an acoustic-quality or speech-API verdict.
- **Integration:** after isolating the compositor, giving the test window actual keyboard focus and cleaning up private test processes, the real host suite passed all 108 tests. The Linux profile uses `--password-store=basic` to avoid a new-keyring prompt. Successful close checks wait for their observable result under the test timeout. Earlier focus, keyring and per-user inotify failures are not acceptance evidence; the final recovery-follow-up run passed 108 (`/tmp/sb-recovery2-integration.log`).
- **Other limits:** the available preview profile had no GitHub sign-in or observed Switchboard marks; no authenticated live board interaction was verified. Spoken enrolment, acoustic barge-in/duplex, lock/sleep transitions and speech-API output remain incomplete. Normal Claude gateway acceptance is separate from direct Code Mode quota and is reported in its own evidence.

## M115. Linux Claude gateway and interrupted-start recovery, 2026-11-20

- **Versions/setup:** Claude Code 2.1.329, Node.js 24.22.1, `claude-haiku-5-5` through the operator's gateway. Separate Switchboard homes, provider config/runtime sockets and scratch repositories; no source polling or writes, operator-service changes or desktop reloads. Evidence: `.wip/linux-acceptance/claude/{gateway,recovery,recovery-followup}/`.
- **Passed:** real ordinary-session reply and file/worktree/stage/done reports with release, question/answer, Stop/resume, hub restart during a turn, failed-turn kind plus message, trust and missing-binary refusals. Promptless Code Mode starts accepted the recorded Linux banner; acting Code Mode turns still require the direct subscription and were quota-blocked.
- **Recovery:** normal, delayed-registration and symlink-root starts survived hub restart and adopted the same native session. An ambiguous label stayed owned with a warning visible on the dock card and refused reports; removing the decoy let recovery adopt the real process, clear its warning and accept reports. Known native/short identities tolerate a renamed label. Unknown labels are not guessed and unrelated processes are never stopped.
- **Constraint:** a permanently absent or unidentified start remains pending. Operator Stop rechecks identity and can clear an unconfirmed ordinary start locally after its warning appears; no unidentified process is stopped. Inspect `claude agents` and stop any leftover process before retrying. The API cleanup passed live: after 122 seconds Stop cleared the failed start and warning, refused late reports, allowed Delete, and allowed a Retry that finished done. An unrelated background session kept running. Ambiguity stays owned until the extra session is removed; local clearing requires a fresh overdue zero-match result. A separate real-hub check generated both recovery controls, invoked their exact actions, verified ambiguity stayed owned and clearing enabled Retry/Delete, refused a late report and removed the deleted session. It used an empty real roster plus injected duplicate entries, no model or credentials. Native confirmation dialogs and pointer clicks were not exercised; the dock and VS Code confirmation consumers were inspected.
- **Limits:** two consecutive blocked/no-PID observations adopt a start prompt, matching ordinary startup; this has unit evidence, not a live MCP approval prompt. Direct Code Mode model turns, spoken voice and authenticated browser-board acceptance remain outside these gateway results.
- **Current use:** PRD R43 startup reconciliation.

## M116. Pierre's CodeView as a many-file view, 2026-11-21

- **Setup:** @pierre/diffs 1.6.4's `CodeView` in headless Chrome through `playwright-cli` at 1,280 by 720 px, with 55 files of this repository's `HEAD~20..HEAD` (482 KB of patch): first a stand-in page using the library directly, then the built page (`page.js` and `dist/diff/`) against a stand-in hub answering each diff after 120 ms, both in a scratch folder of the worktree.
- **Result:** with 3 real items and 52 placeholders the view was 66,596 px tall and mounted only the items on screen (1 at the top, 4 further down, at most 5 while scrolling). `updateItem` with the same id and type and a higher `version` kept the view still when the item replaced was above the view (the visible header stayed at y=0 while `scrollTop` moved 40 px) and when its header was on screen; one whose top was above the view moved the view by one line (20 px), since its fake line was the anchor. `collapsed` took one frame (about 3 ms) either way, a collapsed item being its 44 px header and the 8 px gap; the sticky header is 44 px, Pierre's `diffHeaderHeight`. `loadDiffFiles` ran only when a folded region's expand handle was clicked, never on mount or scroll. While the view scrolls Pierre sets `pointer-events: none` on its sticky container, so a hit test on a header checkbox missed until the checkbox had `pointer-events: auto`. A context-only synthetic patch drew line numbers, Pierre's `-0 +0` and a "More unchanged context may be available" bar, which a whole (`isPartial: false`) patch does not. With the built page, scrolling all 55 files at 1,500 px/s read each file's patch once (`sides=0`), at most 2 reads at once, and no placeholder showed in the view in 11,907 frames; picking the 51st file from the list showed its diff at the top 159 ms later.
- **Not verified:** a real phone, momentum scrolling, a live hub, and the 9,000 px/s frame-time target.
- **Current use:** the web page's Changes tab ([architecture.md](architecture.md) § Web page, Diffs). Re-run after upgrading @pierre/diffs.

## M117. How a transcript records Code Mode, the permission mode and the model, 2026-11-22

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.32x and its VS Code extension, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; the operator's `~/.claude/projects` transcripts, read only.
- **Inspection:** every `.jsonl` under `~/.claude/projects` searched for `"type":"repl-mode"` and `"replMode":false` lines, against those whose lines carry `"entrypoint":"claude-vscode"`; the line types and fields of a Code Mode transcript, a VS Code panel transcript and a background (`sdk-cli`) transcript compared.
- **Result:**
  - A Code Mode session writes `{"type":"repl-mode","replMode":true,"sessionId":…}` lines, each after a `{"type":"mode","mode":"normal",…}` line. 109 transcripts have one; none of the 8,231 VS Code panel transcripts and none of the background or non-Code Mode terminal transcripts sampled do, and no transcript has `"replMode":false`.
  - A terminal session writes `{"type":"permission-mode","permissionMode":…}` lines; a VS Code panel session writes none, but each of its `user` lines carries `permissionMode`. The values seen are `auto`, `bypassPermissions`, `dontAsk`, `plan` and, in older transcripts, `default`, the mode `--permission-mode` now calls `manual`.
  - Each `assistant` line's `message.model` names the model, such as `claude-opus-5-5`; Claude's own notes have `<synthetic>`.
- **Not verified:** whether a session that left Code Mode would write `"replMode":false`; Linux.
- **Current use:** `transcriptLaunch`, which picks how an ended registered session resumes (architecture.md § Code Mode sessions, Registered sessions).

## M118. A queued notice Claude never received, 2026-11-22

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.327 to 2.1.330 on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; one of the operator's terminal transcripts, read only, and strings in the 2.1.330 binary.
- **Inspection:** the transcript's `queue-operation` lines counted by operation and fields; the lines around a stopped-agents notice that a resumed session's conversation showed as an operator message dated three days earlier; the `enqueuePendingNotification` calls in the binary.
- **Result:**
  - When the operator stops background agents, Claude Code queues `2 background agents were stopped by the user: "…", "…".` or `Background agent "…" was stopped by the user.` as a `task-notification`, followed by a `system` line with subtype `agents_killed`. The queue line records only `type`, `operation`, `timestamp`, `sessionId` and `content`, as every one of the 22,016 `enqueue` lines with content does, so it does not say that the entry is a notification. Several other notifications are queued as plain text too, such as Ultraplan's and Ultrareview's.
  - A notification Claude takes is recorded as a `queued_command` attachment with `commandMode` `task-notification` and `origin` `{"kind":"task-notification"}` (128 seen). This one was never taken: the run exited right after it, and the next run, three days later, recorded no `dequeue`, `remove` or receipt for it. A queue entry left at exit is not carried into the resumed run.
  - Claude Code also removes a queued message with `reason` `delivered_to_agent` (13 seen) when it goes to another agent, and the main conversation never receives it.
- **Not verified:** whether Claude Code ever takes the operator's queued messages out of order.
- **Current use:** the stopped-agents notice is left out of the conversation, and a queued message still waiting when a message sent after it is received is withdrawn (architecture.md § Reading a session).

## M119. Resuming a session that ran without Code Mode with Code Mode on, 2026-11-22

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.330 (native build), Haiku 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300; node-pty 1.1.0 and @xterm/headless at 120 × 40, as the holder runs a session.
- **Experiment:** in a scratch git repository with the operator's `~/.claude`, a session was started without Code Mode (`claude --session-id <uuid> --permission-mode auto --model haiku --settings {"tui":"default"} <prompt with a code word>`), with Claude's parent-session markers removed from the environment, and its process ended after the reply. It was then resumed as a Code Mode resume runs (`claude --resume <uuid>` with `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE=1`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` set to the direct API and `codeModeSettings()`), with a prompt asking for the code word and a shell command; then once more with `--fork-session` added.
- **Result:**
  - Both resumes started, kept the conversation (the code word came back) and left the transcript readable, but neither turned Code Mode on: the banner had no `Code Mode (experimental) is on` line, Claude printed `Code Mode is on, but this session isn't using it, so it continues with the ordinary tools. New sessions use Code Mode.`, the model called `PowerShell` directly rather than `REPL`, and neither transcript, the resumed one or the fork's new one, got a `repl-mode` line.
  - With M68, which records the refusal to resume a Code Mode session without Code Mode, Claude keeps a session in the mode it started in, either way; only a new session starts in Code Mode.
- **Not verified:** Linux; a session started in the VS Code panel.
