  - Until the holder gave held sessions `TERM` (holder started 2026-11-24T02:01Z), Claude Code drew the prompt as ">" and hub.log shows "no input box on the screen" for each message, so the draft guard never saw the box. From then on the box was read, and a suggestion shown after a reply blocked typing.
- **Not verified:** a session started through the holder with Switchboard's `--settings`; Linux.
- **Current use:** every `hub.js` command drops `NO_COLOR` and `FORCE_COLOR` from its own environment at startup, so nothing a hub starts inherits them, and `codeModeEnvironment` drops them again for held Claude sessions, so they draw their placeholder and suggestion dim (architecture.md § Hub lifecycle). A held session started before this keeps them until its process ends and is resumed. The "a message waits, the input box holds a draft" log line carries the draft, cut to 60 characters.

## M150. Scroll writes and the top bars' hit test on an emulated iPhone, 2026-11-24

- **Versions:** Playwright 1.64 WebKit (build 2368) headless on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300, as Playwright's `iPhone 15 Pro` device (`isMobile`, `hasTouch`) at 393 by 793; Switchboard at `eb902524`.
- **Experiment:** the built page with `check-shell.mjs`'s fake hub and the installed app's rules. For each top bar control, `document.elementFromPoint` at its centre and `touchscreen.tap` there: on the list, and on a conversation also while typing in the composer and after blurring it (the list's search box was hidden, so nothing was typed there); `window.scrollY`, `visualViewport.offsetTop` and `document.scrollingElement.scrollTop` read alongside. Then, with 300 Done rows open, the list scrolled up 150 px a frame for 40 frames from its end, twice (the second pass over rows already measured), with every `scrollTop` write the page made recorded.
- **Result:**
  - Every control hit itself and every tap reached it, and the scroll offsets stayed 0. Headless WebKit shows no software keyboard, so typing moved no viewport, and the operator's iPhone fault, taps on a top bar landing on the row below it, did not reproduce. [WebKit bug 297779](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=297779) reports iOS 26 leaving `visualViewport.offsetTop` at about 24 px after the keyboard closes, with fixed and sticky bars out of line, and fixes elsewhere scroll the page a pixel and back once the keyboard has closed; `keyboardViewport.ts`, since removed (M155), did the same 300 ms after a visual viewport resize or scroll, a `focusout` or a `pageshow`, when the viewport is within 120 px of `innerHeight`, unzoomed, and `offsetTop` or `scrollY` is not 0. In the headless page the document had no scroll range, so both `scrollTo` calls left it at 0.
  - Before the fix, Done's windowing (`windowRows.tsx`) wrote `scrollTop` on 39 of 40 frames of the second pass, each write the offset already there; the first pass wrote 101 times, 37 of them unchanged. After it, the second pass wrote none and the first wrote 58 times, each a real shift from a row measuring other than its 72 px estimate.
- **Not verified:** anything on an iPhone: the keyboard, whether WebKit bug 297779 causes the fault, whether the realignment clears it, and a fling's bounce, where iOS reports a negative offset that a write clamps to 0.
- **Current use:** Done's windowing, which writes only a real shift inside the scroll range (architecture.md § Web page). `keyboardViewport.ts` was removed after M155.

## M151. gh and git in Codex's Windows sandbox, 2026-11-24

- **Versions:** Codex CLI 0.171.0, Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300, the operator's Codex config with `[windows] sandbox = "elevated"` and `shell_environment_policy.inherit = "core"`; gh signed in to the keyring.
- **Experiment:** `codex exec` in a scratch git repository (`.wip/gh-sandbox`) with `approval_policy="never"`, `sandbox_mode="workspace-write"` and network access on, so every command stayed in the sandbox `auto` uses; the prompt ran `gh api user`, `gh auth status`, `git ls-remote` on a public and a private repository and `curl.exe` to api.github.com.
- **Result:**
  - As configured, `gh` answered 401 (`The token in default is invalid`), and `git` and `curl.exe` failed TLS with schannel's `SEC_E_NO_CREDENTIALS`: the elevated sandbox runs commands as a separate user, which can read neither the operator's keyring nor use schannel.
  - With `GH_TOKEN` set in the environment Codex started with and passed through (`shell_environment_policy.inherit = "all"` with `ignore_default_excludes = true`, or an `include_only` list naming it), `gh api user` answered the operator's login. `git -c http.sslBackend=openssl` read the public repository, and with `-c credential.helper= -c "credential.helper=!gh auth git-credential"` the private one too. `curl.exe` still failed.
  - With `windows.sandbox = "unelevated"`, `gh` read the keyring and worked without `GH_TOKEN`, while git and `curl.exe` still failed schannel, and commands through the WindowsApps `pwsh.exe` alias failed to start (`CreateProcessAsUserW failed: 5`); through `cmd.exe` they ran.
- **Not verified:** a `git push`; PowerShell web calls, .NET and NuGet, which use schannel and are expected to fail the same way; Node's `fetch`; the app-server path Switchboard's sessions use, which runs the same sandbox.
- **Current use:** PRD R22 and the Settings help for Codex's permission mode: Switchboard passes no credentials, so `gh` and `git` in an `auto` Codex session need the operator's Codex shell environment and git settings above. Supersedes M31's conclusion that such skills run only in `bypassPermissions`.

## M152. Clipped website frames pass the pointer to the page under them, 2026-11-24

- **Setup:** Windows, headless Chrome 158 through playwright-cli, a scratch fixture of three loopback listeners on different origins: an outer page in the place of the extension host, a full-viewport child in the place of the Switchboard page with a menu, a modal backdrop and a dialog, and a website frame placed over the child by the branch's bundled `IframeFrames`. No extension, hub or website was involved. Scratch fixture under the session scratchpad.
- **Result:** with an even-odd `clip-path: path(...)` cutting the menu's box out of the website frame, a click inside the cut reached the child's menu, a click on the frame beside it reached the website, and a click just outside a rounded cut corner reached the website. The cut showed the child's menu. With the frame inert, `pointer-events: none` and `filter: brightness(0.6)`, a click on the dialog's cut reached the dialog, a click on the darkened website reached the child's backdrop, and the website rendered darkened. Clearing the styles returned clicks to the website.
- **Live acceptance, 2026-11-24:** with the hub and extension installed from main, the operator confirmed on a GitHub tab that the page's menus and dialogs draw over the website and take the pointer.
- **Not established:** branded Edge, websites other than GitHub, a website that captures the pointer, and touch input.
- **Current use:** PRD R125's website tabs under the page's layers (architecture.md § Embedded link tabs).

## M153. Service-worker navigations escape the frame's header rule, 2026-11-24

- **Setup:** Windows, Playwright's Chromium 155 with the unpacked extension in an isolated persistent profile, signed in to the live hub page and, by the operator, to Zoho Desk. Work item 679, a Zoho ticket. Read-only: no ticket was changed.
- **Before:** the ticket URL `desk.zoho.com/support/<portal>/ShowHomePage.do` loaded in the website frame, then Zoho navigated the frame to `desk.zoho.com/agent/...`, which showed "desk.zoho.com refused to connect". That response came from Zoho's service worker (`/agent/_sw-v3.js`, which answers navigations with a network fetch) and kept `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN`, although the session rule for the tab and `desk.zoho.com` sub_frames was in place. Signed out, Zoho instead sends the frame to `accounts.zoho.com`, which refuses framing too.
- **Rule boundaries:** a temporary rule for `desk.zoho.com` with no tab (-1) and the `xmlhttprequest` type let the page load; the `other` or `sub_frame` type did not. With the extension's host setting the frame's source, the worker fetch matched the extension as top domain and initiator; with Zoho navigating its own frame, it matched only `desk.zoho.com` as top domain and initiator. Removing only X-Frame-Options was enough, since Zoho's CSP has no frame-ancestors. With the rule in place, an inline script without a nonce still did not run in the framed page nor in an ordinary Zoho tab, so both kept their CSP. Chrome documents that a request without a top-level frame takes its top domain from its initiator, so the rule cannot tell this frame from the same host framed under another site; not tested live.
- **Not established:** other sites with service workers, a CSP frame-ancestors refusal served from a service worker, and Edge. Zoho's own nested widgets on other Zoho hosts (`tipengine.zoho.com`, `avcliq.zoho.com`) still refuse framing, as nested cross-site frames do by design.
- **Testing note:** Chromium keeps an unpacked extension's cached service-worker script when it is loaded again with the same manifest version from the command line, so a test copy needs a new version; `chrome://extensions` Reload does not have this problem.
- **Current use:** `iframeWorkerRule` in the Chrome extension, only for the hosts in `IFRAME_WORKER_HOSTS` (architecture.md § Embedded link tabs).

## M154. A WebSocket from the extension's frame goes without the SameSite sign-in cookie, 2026-11-24

- **Setup:** Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300, Playwright's Chromium 155 (`chromium-1247`) and Microsoft Edge 158.0.4370.33, each in a scratch profile with a minimal unpacked Manifest V3 extension (host permissions `http://*/*` and `https://*/*`, loaded with `--load-extension` and `--disable-features=DisableLoadExtensionCommandLineSwitch`). A test-owned HTTPS server on `127.0.0.1:8443`, mapped to `sb.test` with `--host-resolver-rules`, set `sb_page=abc; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/` from a tab, then served a page that posted `fetch` to its own origin and opened `wss://sb.test:8443/ws`. That page loaded once as a tab and once in an iframe of an extension page; the server logged each request's `Cookie` and `Origin`. Harness: `.wip/voice-ext-ws/`.
- **Evidence:** the operator's `hub.log` on 2026-11-24 and 25 showed Mic on taps from the paired Surface's Edge page failing with "Voice did not start." after the microphone was allowed, with no voice socket opened, while the same device's earlier taps from an ordinary tab opened it.
- **Result:** in both browsers the tab's document, fetch and WebSocket carried the cookie. In the extension's frame the document and fetch carried it, through Chromium's extension cookie exemption, but the WebSocket upgrade carried no cookie; its `Origin` was the frame's own origin, `https://sb.test:8443`. The hub answered such an upgrade 401 "Not signed in.", which the page sees only as a close with no reason.
- **Not established:** a real `tailscale serve`, Firefox, and whether `SameSite=None` would be sent there under Edge's tracking prevention; it was not tried.
- **Current use:** the voice ticket (architecture.md § Remote access, Voice on a phone).

## M155. The installed app's viewport while typing in the composer on an iPhone, 2026-11-25

- **Versions:** the operator's iPhone, the page installed to the Home Screen (standalone), portrait; Switchboard `feat/doc-tabs` at `978df738` with a temporary trace that sent `visualViewport`, `innerHeight`, `scrollY`, the conversation's `scrollTop` and the composer's box to the hub's log on each key, the three frames after it, each viewport or window scroll and resize, and each decision of `keyboardViewport.ts`.
- **Experiment:** the operator focused the composer, typed a line, and blurred it, three times.
- **Result:**
  - With the keyboard closed: `visualViewport.height` 694, `innerHeight` 762, `scrollY` 0.
  - On focus, in one step: `visualViewport.height` 357 and `innerHeight` 357, so `innerHeight` shrinks with the keyboard in the installed app; `scrollY` and `visualViewport.offsetTop` 405, the document scrolled to keep the composer above the keyboard (its top at 249.7 of 357). Keys moved none of these.
  - `keyboardViewport.ts`'s test for an open keyboard, `visualViewport.height < innerHeight - 120`, read false while the keyboard showed, and its other test read the page out of line (`scrollY` 405), so before `978df738` it scrolled the window to 0 after each viewport event, putting the composer back under the keyboard: the jump the operator saw from `b612cdd8` (2026-11-24) on.
  - On blur, each time: `scrollY` and `offsetTop` back to 0 with nothing done by the page. The out-of-line viewport of WebKit bug 297779 (M150) did not occur.
- **Not verified:** iPadOS, landscape, and Safari outside the installed app.
- **Current use:** the page does not scroll the window (architecture.md § Web page, iPhone and iPad).

## M156. GitHub's issue and pull request layout, 2026-11-25

- **Setup:** Playwright's Chromium in a signed-in profile at 1280 and 1600 CSS pixels wide, on a private repository's issue page and on each tab of an open pull request (Overview, Commits, Checks, Files changed). A script listed each element with a `max-width` of 700 px or more and the ancestors of the sidebar and of the global header's menu button; then CSS injected into the page hid the sidebar and set those `max-width`s to `none`.
- **Result:** both pages are React apps under the same global header, whose left group (`data-testid="top-nav-left"`) holds the menu button (`octicon-three-bars`), its tooltip and the home link. An issue's sidebar is `data-testid="issue-viewer-metadata-container"` (296 px at 1600 px); its content is capped at 1280 px by `contentContainer__<hash>`. Below a narrower window width GitHub moves the issue's metadata above the body instead (`issueMetadata__<hash>`). A pull request's sidebar, only on Overview, is the `prc-PageLayout-PaneWrapper-<hash>` holding `data-testid="sticky-sidebar"` (320 px); the Overview body is capped at 1012 px (`prc-PageLayout-ContentWrapper-` and `prc-PageLayout-Content-`), Commits and Checks at 1280 px (`constrainedContent__<hash>`) and every tab's header at 1372 px (`constrainedHeader__<hash>`); Files changed has no cap. With the CSS injected, the body filled the window on every page and nothing else moved. The PR header's "Open panel" button opens GitHub's own side panel, not this sidebar. The built extension, loaded unpacked, then kept both settings across a reload, an issue and a pull request tab reached by a click, and removed them on the pull request list. A pull request's tab row (`prc-PageHeader-Navigation-<hash>`, holding the `UnderlineNav`) is the same on all four tabs; its list spans the row's full width inside a `nav` at `z-index: 11`, so an element pinned to the row's right end needs a higher `z-index` to take clicks. An issue has no tab row; its header is `data-testid="issue-header"`, whose right column below the New issue button is empty and sits above the sidebar.
- **Not established:** Firefox, GitHub's narrow and mobile layouts, and GitHub Enterprise.
- **Current use:** the issue and pull request layout (architecture.md § Browser extension; PRD R127). The fixtures `extensions/chrome/test/fixtures/layout-*.html` record the markup.

## M157. The GitHub layout buttons in an embedded link tab, 2026-11-25

- **Setup:** Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300, Playwright's Chromium (`chromium-1247`) in a scratch profile with the unpacked build of `fix/github-layout-frame` loaded by `--load-extension` and `--disable-features=DisableLoadExtensionCommandLineSwitch`, signed out of GitHub. A session rule like `iframeRule` removed `X-Frame-Options` and `Content-Security-Policy` from github.com sub-frames under the extension. A tab opened a public issue (`microsoft/vscode` #100000) or pull request (#200000); then the extension's `iframeHost.html` got a frame with `IframeFrames`' `sandbox`, `allow` and `referrerPolicy`, 1420 or 900 px wide, on the same URL. The real host's startup, which needs a hub, was not used. Harness: `.wip/frame-repro/`.
- **Before (build of 85c822b6):** in the tab the buttons showed and Full width set its attribute and `localStorage`. In the frame the page had GitHub's issue header and read the tab's `localStorage` value, but had no buttons and no `data-switchboard-*` attribute: neither content script ran, since a manifest content script without `all_frames` runs only in the top frame. The same build with `all_frames: true` on both entries showed the buttons in the frame and applied the tab's saved setting.
- **After (layout script and `content.css` in every frame, board script top-frame only):** at 1420 and 900 px, clicking Full width and Hide sidebar in the frame set both attributes there, hid the sidebar (296 or 256 px wide before, 0 after) and saved the settings, and the tab followed at once through the `storage` event; a click in the tab undid Full width in the frame the same way. So the frame's github.com `localStorage` is not partitioned from the tab's under the extension's host permission, and the 900 px frame kept GitHub's sidebar layout. On the signed-out pull request both the tab and the frame hid the Hide sidebar button, since that page has no `sticky-sidebar`.
- **Not established:** a signed-in GitHub session in the frame, the real host started by the web page, Edge, and Firefox, which has no embedded link tabs.
- **Current use:** the issue and pull request layout in embedded link tabs (architecture.md § Browser extension; PRD R127).

## M158. Where the Changes tab's diffs spend their time, 2026-11-25

- **Setup:** Windows 11, git for Windows, Node.js 24, headless Chrome through the DevTools protocol at 1,400 by 900 px. A test-owned repository: 19,000 tracked C# and TypeScript files, `origin/main` as base, 3 commits of 30 files, 5 staged, 5 unstaged and 5 untracked files and a 3,000-line file with 427 lines changed (33 rows); a second worktree with one commit of 300 changed files. The hub's `PageChanges` with every git invocation timed, alone and behind a stand-in server serving the built page and diff viewer with a fake stream naming the repository. The machine was running other builds throughout, so one git invocation took 40 ms to 1.5 s; the base build (`85c822b6`) and the changed one ran in turn, three rounds each, and the ranges below are over those rounds. The scripts are in `.wip/diff-load/` of the `perf/diff-load` worktree.
- **Before:** each `diff` and `file` request ran the whole change read again, 12 git invocations (`rev-parse` twice, `merge-base`, `log`, `rev-list`, five `git diff` and `ls-files --others` over the worktree, `hash-object` of every row's file), then the patch's own `git diff`, 30 to 400 ms. One diff took 0.5 to 6.3 s; 12 diffs read three at a time, as the page reads them, 4.7 to 25.3 s and 156 invocations, the three reads at once each scanning the worktree. On the page: a first open showed its first diff 3.5 to 6.2 s after the tab opened, a reload 2.1 to 8.5 s, a jump to a far file 2.1 to 4.8 s, the 300-file change 3.6 to 11.3 s; scrolling it at 6,000 px/s left placeholders on screen 14 to 34 s in all over 12 s, up to 4.2 s for one file.
- **Not the cause:** the viewer's modules (`diff.js` 414 KB, 267 KB gzipped with its chunks) load while the list is read and are cached across reloads; the worker pool is one per page and keeps its grammars. Highlighting never held back a diff: Pierre draws it uncoloured 25 to 65 ms after its answer and colours it when a worker answers, 0.1 to 0.2 s later for a small file and 1.1 to 1.3 s for the 3,000-line one. No task over 58 ms was seen. Choosing the tab again drew the held diffs in 20 to 35 ms, but gave each a new cache key, so the 3,000-line file was coloured again 1.0 to 1.2 s later.
- **After** (the page's last read of its changes reused while HEAD, the base refs, the index file and each row's paths' working tree changes are as it read them, checked by `git status` for those paths alone, `for-each-ref` and a `stat` of the index; the change read's first steps side by side; a diff's cache key kept for its read; the next three files read ahead): one diff 0.08 to 1.0 s with 3 or 4 invocations, `git status` for one path 40 to 290 ms; 12 diffs 1.2 to 3.1 s with 36; the list 0.4 to 1.5 s against 0.5 to 3.4 s before. On the page: first diff 3.2 to 4.0 s after a first open against 4.0 to 4.9 s in the same rounds, most of it the list and git under load; a reload 1.6 to 3.1 s against 3.1 to 7.9 s; a jump 0.4 to 2.3 s against 2.8 to 3.9 s; the 300-file change 0.85 to 1.2 s against 5.0 to 5.9 s; the tab chosen again coloured at once (20 to 28 ms); placeholders on screen 0 to 0.4 s in all while scrolling, at most 0.2 s for one file, against 14 to 29 s and 1.0 to 4.1 s. The measured build compared the index by size and time and also reused a list at its row limit; the shipped one adds the index's file ID, compares its time to the nanosecond and reads a list at its row limit again, which costs no git invocation.
- **Read-ahead:** before that run, with the diff reads already reused, reading the next three files rather than the next one, two rounds each in turn, left placeholders on screen 4.2 and 0 s in all against 8.3 and 5.1 s, at most 0.7 s against 0.8 s.
- **Not verified:** a real large checkout and the live hub, a phone or a remote device over Tailscale, where the viewer's modules' transfer may count, and a quiet machine.
- **Current use:** the Changes tab's diff and file reads ([architecture.md](architecture.md) § Web page, Diffs) and `READ_AHEAD` in `packages/web/src/changesWindow.ts`.

## M159. A paste of image paths becomes images in the message, 2026-11-25

- **Versions:** Claude Code CLI 2.1.332, Opus 5.5, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26300.
- **Source:** the operator's held Code Mode session's transcript, read after a message typed from the web page stayed provisional, and the hub's log of its typing. No experiment was run.
- **Result:** the page pasted one bracketed paste: a line of text ending in a quoted image path, then two lines that each held only a quoted absolute path to a PNG. Claude Code attached the two files as images and removed their lines; the path inside the first line stayed text. The user line (`promptSource: "typed"`, `imagePasteIds: [4, 5]`) held one text block, `[Image #4] [Image #5]` followed by the first line, then the two `image` blocks; an `isMeta` companion line written at the same time named each image as `[Image: source: <path>]`. The placeholders went where the paste began, with a space between them and none before the text. The input box was empty afterwards, so the hub counted the message sent, but its text matched no transcript line.
- **Not established:** a paste whose path lines name a file that cannot be read, other image types, unquoted paths, a path pasted between other lines, and the queue line of such a paste typed while Claude works.
- **Current use:** a typed message matches a line whose placeholders replace as many path-only lines of its text, with the rest of the text the same (architecture.md § Typing a message, Matching); `packages/conversation/test/fixtures/transcripts/image-paste.jsonl` records the lines, scrubbed. M86 and M88 record earlier paste shapes.

## M160. The Wayland app ID of the extension's app window on Linux, 2026-11-25

- **Setup:** Ubuntu 26.04.1, GNOME on Wayland, Google Chrome 158.0.8127.88 running natively on Wayland (its default there). Chrome was started with `WAYLAND_DEBUG=1`, a scratch `--user-data-dir`, `--profile-directory` set to `Default`, `Profile 1`, `Profile 2` and `my-prof.x`, and `--app=chrome-extension://hlbibnhgkgnjkbfledhgjblacloneije/iframeHost.html?origin=…`; the protocol log recorded each `xdg_toplevel.set_app_id`. The extension was not installed, so the window showed an error page; the app ID depends only on the URL and the profile.
- **Result:** the app ID was `chrome-hlbibnhgkgnjkbfledhgjblacloneije__iframeHost.html-<profile>`, with each space in the profile directory replaced by `_` and other characters, such as `-` and `.`, kept: `…-Default`, `…-Profile_1`, `…-my-prof.x`. The query string does not change it, so two hubs in one profile share it, as they share a Windows app identity. GNOME matches a window to a launcher whose `StartupWMClass` equals its app ID. For comparison, the page installed as a Chrome web app opens with `--app-id=<id>` and gets the app ID `chrome-<id>-Default`.
- **Not established:** Chrome under X11 (its `WM_CLASS`), Chromium, Edge, Flatpak or Snap builds of Chrome, a non-default `--user-data-dir` for the real profile, and desktops other than GNOME.
- **Current use:** the Linux app launcher (architecture.md § Browser extension; PRD R125).
